# **Our Guiding Force...** 



#### _Late Shri. Rasiklal Raveshia Late Shri. Mansukhlal Rajani_ 

We extend our deepest gratitude for the visionary leadership and enduring passion of our founders, whose pioneering spirit has shaped our company into the thriving organisation it is today. Their bold vision, relentless drive and resolute focus have established the groundwork for our achievements, inspiring generations to pursue excellence. 

Starting with a modest facility, they aimed for distinction in every aspect of the business from the very beginning. As true visiona ~~ri~~ es and technocrats, they brought exceptional expertise to the manufacturing process. Their emphasis on innovation, paired with their dynamic personalities and sound governance, has directed the course of DOMS to the loved brand it is today. 

Our founders possessed remarkable foresight in selecting an industry primed for growth and transformation. DOMS is forever indebted to their contributions and strives to honour their legacy by pushing boundaries, exploring new frontiers and making a meaningful impact. 

Today, as we look to the future, we draw strength from the foundation laid by our founders. The legacy of our founders serves as a constant source of motivation, reminding us of the power of innovation, perseverance and collaboration. At the same time, we always aim to remain rooted in the core values and principles that our founders instilled. 

Thank you to our founders for your enduring legacy, which inspires us every day to reach new heights! 

## **Etched in Our Hearts Forever...** 



### _Late Shri. Amarjeet Sharma (1954 – 2024)_ 

We cherish the memory of Shri. Amarjeet Sharma, a remarkable leader who played an instrumental role in shaping the DOMS success story. His unwavering dedication, strategic vision and profound commitment to excellence have left an incredible mark on our Company. Shri. Amarjeet Sharma was admired for his foresight, sales astuteness and business acumen, not only at DOMS, but throughout the Indian Stationery industry. 

He was known for his jovial nature and ability to connect with everyone, making him one of the most loved people in our lives. While his absence leaves a deep void, his legacy continues to inspire us all. Let us honour his memory by continuing to uphold the vision he had for DOMS – ‘to make “DOMS” the most loved brand’!

Every meaningful achievement begins long before the outcome becomes visible. It is shaped by disciplined planning, thoughtful choices and the willingness to invest consistently in the future. Whether nurturing creativity, building capabilities or expanding opportunities, enduring progress is created through deliberate actions taken today. 

This belief defines the way we conduct our business. Every product we develop, every innovation we pursue and every investment we make reflects our commitment to building a stronger foundation for tomorrow. From expanding manufacturing capabilities and strengthening our distribution network to advancing digital integration and developing our people, each initiative is designed to enhance resilience, create lasting value and support sustainable growth. 

The same philosophy guides our purpose of enabling learning, creativity and self-expression. By providing trusted products that accompany children through every stage of their journey, we help transform curiosity into confidence and potential into achievement. 

As we continue to grow, we remain focused on building capabilities, embracing change and preparing for emerging opportunities. Through this disciplined approach, we strengthen our ability to create enduring value for customers, partners, communities and shareholders alike.

### **About us** 

# Where Growth Takes Shape 

For over two decades, DOMS has empowered learning, creativity and self-expression.  Every ambition we serve begins with thoughtful preparation and purpose. Our customers place their trust in our diverse product portfolio. Today, we are one of India’s leading branded stationery and art material companies, with a growing presence across the kids centric consumer ecosystem through a diversified portfolio that includes school and office stationery, art materials, paper stationery, hobby and craft products, toys, school bags and baby hygiene products. Every offering is designed with a focus on quality, functionality, and a meaningful user experience. 

Our strength lies in disciplined preparation across design, development and manufacturing. We integrate strong manufacturing capabilities with deep product development expertise and a clear understanding of evolving consumer needs. This enables us to serve students, professionals, artists and families effectively, while ensuring consistent quality, faster product readiness and the agility to respond to changing market expectations. 

As we expand across the kids-centric consumer ecosystem, we remain focused on building trusted brands, strengthening consumer connections and creating lasting value for all stakeholders. Guided by quality, design excellence and operational strength, we continue to scale responsibly and shape the next phase of growth with intent and discipline.

## **Awards and Certifications** 

# Built on Scale, Innovation and Reach 

A diversified product portfolio, integrated manufacturing capabilities, focused innovation and a strong distribution network enable us to meet evolving customer needs while delivering consistent quality and sustainable growth. 

### **Diversified Product Portfolio** 

Our broad portfolio across Scholastic Stationery, Scholastic Art Material, Kits & Combos, Office Supplies, Paper Stationery, Hobby & Craft, Fine Art Products, Back to School Products and Hygiene Products categories enables us to address evolving customer preferences and capture new growth opportunities. 





### **Modern, Scalable and Integrated Manufacturing Infrastructure** 

Our integrated manufacturing network and backward integration enhance quality, improve operational efficiencies and provide the flexibility to support growing demand. 



### **Focused R&D and Product Engineering** 

Our R&D and product engineering capabilities enable us to develop differentiated products that combine innovation, quality and functionality. 



### **Multi Channel Distribution Network** 

A widespread presence across general trade, modern retail and ecommerce ensures extensive market reach and consistent product availability across India. 



### **Deep Consumer Reach and Strong Brand Connect** 

Our portfolio of trusted brands, expanding consumer reach and strong channel partnerships strengthen brand recall and support long term growth.

## **Management Message** 

# Creating Possibilities, Inspiring Ambitions 

### **Dear Shareholders,** 

It is a pleasure to present our Annual Report for the financial year 2025-26.

#### **A Portfolio Built for Every Stage** 

Our domestic business continued to anchor our performance, contributing 87.5% of gross product sales, while exports accounted for the remaining 12.5% and recorded steady double-digit growth. 

At DOMS, we believe every ambition needs preparation. This belief shapes the way we design, develop and deliver products that inspire learning, nurture creativity and enable self-expression. Every solution we create is thoughtfully engineered to serve the evolving needs of students, professionals, artists and institutions, combining purposeful design with precision manufacturing and uncompromising quality. 

We view our expansion beyond stationery into a wider kids, children, young adults and professionals consumer products business not as diversification, but as a natural extension of the trust that generations of parents, students and educators have placed in the DOMS brand. This enables us to support every stage of a child’s learning journey while further consolidating our leadership in stationery and art material industry. 

Driven by this philosophy, during the financial year 2025-26, we showcased the breadth of our product portfolio, powered by advanced manufacturing and an unwavering focus on research, quality and sustainability. More importantly, it reflected how we transform ideas into thoughtfully designed tools that inspire confidence, encourage creativity and enhance everyday performance. 

During the year, we strengthened our portfolio with the introduction of wide range of adhesives, tin pencil boxes, well-designed school bags and new range of paper stationery products with fresh designs, a exciting range of pens, mechanical pencils, stamp pads, kits, combos and scholastic art products. These additions were driven by a research and design team of over 60 professionals and supported a quality assurance team of more than 175, ensuring every product reflects our commitment to functionality, reliability and quality. 

For us, innovation is meaningful only when it enriches the experience of those who use our products. As expectations evolve, we remain focused on anticipating changing needs, refining our capabilities and creating solutions that make learning, working and creating more rewarding. This purpose continues to guide our journey as we empower individuals to realise their potential, because every aspiration deserves the right beginning.

Our disciplined approach to category building continued to deliver encouraging results. Today, our portfolio spans nine product categories and more than 4,800 SKUs, providing us with a broad platform to address diverse customer needs. 

Our domestic business continued to anchor our performance, contributing 87.5% of gross product sales, while exports accounted for the remaining 12.5% and recorded steady double-digit growth. Looking ahead, GST rate rationalisation announced in the Union Budget are expected to support consumer demand and accelerate the shift towards the organised sector. #### **Advancing Amid Evolving Market Dynamics** 

Against a backdrop of evolving market dynamics, financial year 2025-26 was another year of resilient, growth-led performance for DOMS. The operating environment was influenced by GST rate rationalisation and global trade uncertainties 

#### **Growth that Speaks Through Numbers** 

Performance during the year was broadbased across our portfolio. Categories 

supported by capacity expansions recorded particularly strong momentum, while established categories also delivered healthy numbers through product enhancements and improved average selling prices. This balanced performance was underpinned by robust domestic demand, our extensive distribution network, strong brand equity and a well-diversified product portfolio. Our export business also maintained steady double-digit growth despite global trade uncertainties, including trade tensions, geopolitical conflicts and regional instability, reflecting the resilience of demand for our products across international markets. 

Revenue from operations grew 21.6% to ₹ 2,32,636.52 lakhs, driven by sustained category expansion, new product launches and strong domestic demand. EBITDA increased 15.5% to ₹ 40,259.62 lakhs, with a margin of 17.3%. The moderation in margin compared to financial year 202425 was primarily attributable to the higher contribution of Uniclan to our consolidated operations. Profit after tax grew 12.2% to ₹23,956.25 lakhs, with a PAT margin of 10.3%. The PAT margin compared to financial year 2024-25 was impacted by lower finance income resulting from higher utilisation of funds towards capital expenditure. 

#### **Strategic Alliances, Shared Ambitions** 

Our investments in adjacent businesses and strategic partnerships continue to broaden our capabilities while creating new avenues for us. Skido Industries Private Limited remained in its learning phase after its first introductory back-to-school season last year, with full-year revenue increasing from ₹ 895.64 lakhs to ₹ 1,408.02 lakhs. During the year, backward integration for kits and combo packs enhanced operational efficiencies while supporting the business as it strengthens its presence in the domestic market. In addition, our acquisition of a 51% stake in Super Treads Private Limited has further strengthened our paper stationery capabilities and expanded our presence in Eastern India. 

Our baby hygiene subsidiary, Uniclan, also delivered encouraging progress, with fullyear revenue of around ₹ 20,309.59 lakhs, 

up 22.3% over its base year. Our strategy remains centred on expanding its market presence through carefully selected channel partners, while driving stronger secondary sales and improving throughput across the existing network. 

portfolio by leveraging the heritage and recall of a recognised legacy brand. 

The acquisition also enhances our sales capabilities, enabling wider market reach and deeper customer engagement. It creates opportunities to introduce products across multiple categories under the Reynolds brand while expanding our channel partner network, supporting long term growth across the writing instrument segment. 

During the year, we increased our stake in Pioneer Stationery Private Limited from 51% to 64%, reinforcing our position in the paper stationery segment. This deepened ownership reflects our continued confidence in Pioneer’s business and enables closer integration of its operations with the wider DOMS ecosystem. 

#### **A Bigger Canvas for Bigger Ambitions** 

Our partnership with F.I.L.A. continues to enhance product development while providing access to its global distribution network and exclusive marketing rights for F.I.L.A. Group products across seven countries. Although intercompany exports were impacted during the year by US tariff actions and softer demand in Europe, the easing of tariffs, and the development of new capacity, provides a positive outlook for the business. 

Further, the recent acquisition of the Reynolds brand, along with its related assets, presents a significant opportunity to build a parallel brand focused on young adults and professionals. The acquisition expands our manufacturing capabilities through the addition of established assets, while strengthening our writing instruments 

The recent acquisition of the Reynolds brand, along with its related assets, presents a significant opportunity to build a parallel brand focused on young adults and professionals. The acquisition expands our manufacturing capabilities through the addition of established assets, while strengthening our writing instruments portfolio by leveraging the heritage and recall of a recognised legacy brand. The acquisition also enhances our sales capabilities, enabling wider market reach and deeper customer engagement. It creates opportunities to introduce products across multiple categories under the Reynolds brand while expanding our channel partner network, supporting long term growth across the writing instrument segment.

Supporting these tailwinds is an extensive distribution network. Today, DOMS reaches the market through more than 130 super stockists, more than 6,250 distributors, over 1,45,000 retail outlets and a sales force of over 1,000. Uniclan complements this footprint with a network of more than 95 super stockists, more than 1,300 distributors and over 55,000 retail outlets and a sales force of over 210. 

**Where Ideas Take Shape** 

As our business continues to expand, strengthening our manufacturing capabilities remains essential to meeting rising demand while preserving the quality, efficiency and reliability that define the DOMS brand. The company totally spent around ₹ 29,200 lakhs towards these objectives. Our capex was primarily towards the development of our 50+ acre land parcel, acquisition of additional land parcels, both in Umbergaon and Jammu for our future expansion as well as procurement and installation of plant and machinery in the existing infrastructure as well as for the commercialization of the 50+ acre facility. 

As part of the phased development of our approximately 50+ acre manufacturing facility, we have taken possession of the first two buildings. Commercial production is expected to commence towards the end of second quarter of financial year 2026-27. Significant investment were also done in expansion of our moulding capacities, writing instruments as well as in the adhesive manufacturing infrastructure. 

Complementing these investments is our backward-integrated manufacturing network, which today spans 18 facilities covering more than 2 million square feet across five locations. This integrated footprint enhances product quality, improves cost efficiencies, strengthens supply chain resilience and provides a scalable platform to support our long-term growth ambitions.

# **Caring for Communities and the Planet** 

Initiatives at our KidZania painting studios, which engage more than 30,000 children every month at each centre, and participation at stationery exhibitions further enriched these interactions. Supported by data-driven insights and analytics, we 

The trust placed in us by our consumers carries a broader responsibility to contribute positively to society and conduct our operations with care and accountability. During the financial year 2025-26, we incorporated DOMS Foundation to undertake Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) activities, donation, other charitable and not-for-profit activities in accordance with its objects and applicable laws. Our efforts focused on education through special education and vocational skill development programmes, while also supporting women’s empowerment through a mass marriage initiative, thereby contributing to the overall social welfare and community development. We also progressed two long-term projects: an educational institution and a hospital in Umbergaon, the latter being developed in collaboration with the Umargam Industries Association. 

During the financial year 2025-26, we incorporated DOMS Foundation to undertake Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) activities, donation, other charitable and notfor-profit activities in accordance with its objects and applicable laws. 

Our approach continues to be measured and disciplined drawing on past experience in navigating periods of disruption, where a focused and prudent response has supported sustainable growth over time. With our strong brand, distribution reach, new products pipeline and capacity investments underway, we believe we are well positioned to deliver on consistent growth, we remain confident in our outlook and are targeting consolidated revenue growth of 18% to 20% in financial year 2026-27, despite the prevailing uncertainties. 

Our immediate priority is to ensure uninterrupted manufacturing operations and supply chain continuity while carefully managing cost pressures. Since the beginning of the year, input costs linked to crude derivatives have risen and we have responded through calibrated pricing and channel margin actions. During these uncertain times, the Company has prioritized volume-led growth and market share expansion, over near-term margin considerations amid sharp and volatile commodity inflation. 

Across our operations, we increased the use of renewable energy, particularly solar power, improved energy and resource efficiency through smarter manufacturing practices, expanded recycling initiatives and maintained key environmental and quality certifications. We also reinforced our focus on workplace safety through risk assessments, training programmes and awareness initiatives. 

Our approach continues to be measured and disciplined drawing on past experience in navigating periods of disruption, where a focused and prudent response has supported sustainable growth over time. With our strong brand, distribution reach, new products pipeline and capacity investments underway, we believe we are well positioned to deliver on consistent growth, we remain confident in our outlook and are targeting consolidated revenue growth of 18% to 20% in financial year 2026-27, despite the prevailing uncertainties. 



On behalf of the Board and the management team, we extend our sincere gratitude to our employees for their dedication, our distributors, retailers and business partners for their enduring collaboration, our super stockiest, distributors, consumers for the confidence they place in our brands, and our shareholders for their unwavering support. We look to the future with confidence and a shared determination to build on the progress we have achieved together. 

Warm regards, 

**DOMS Team**

### **Manufacturing Excellence** 

# Building Capacity for Tomorrow 



Our integrated manufacturing network provides the scale, flexibility and operational discipline to support a growing and diversified product portfolio. Backed by extensive backward integration and modern production facilities, it strengthens manufacturing capabilities while upholding high standards of quality, efficiency and supply reliability.

## **Brownfield Phased Capacity Expansion Addition** 

#### **Building Capacity for Future Growth** 

The phased development of our greenfield manufacturing complex progressed steadily during the year. Prolonged and unseasonal monsoon conditions resulted in a marginal construction delay. Commercial production from the first building is expected to commence during the second quarter of financial year 2026-27. 

Aligned with Market Demand 

Supporting Near Term Capacity Requirements 



Alongside this project, ongoing brownfield expansion initiatives continue to enhance manufacturing capacity and support our growth ambitions. Designed with a phased implementation approach, the complex provides the flexibility to align future capacity creation with evolving market demand. This measured approach ensures disciplined capital deployment while supporting long-term scalability. 

## **50+  acres** 

Greenfield Manufacturing Complex 

## **Q2 FY27** 

Commercial Production from First Building 

#### **Strengthening Backward Integration** 

Our backward integration strategy strengthens manufacturing capabilities by improving quality, reducing reliance on external suppliers and enhancing operational control. With most key components produced in house, we oversee the value chain from design and manufacturing to packaging and distribution, enabling consistent quality, cost efficiencies and faster response to market requirements. 

During the year, we further enhanced manufacturing processes and supply chain efficiencies, reducing external dependencies while supporting timely delivery of high quality products and strengthening long standing supplier relationships. 



#### **Flexible Capacity Expansion** 

Our capacity expansion strategy remains aligned with evolving market demand and business priorities. During the year, we expanded manufacturing capacity for ball point pens, paper stationery, mechanical pencils and adhesives within our existing facilities to address strong market demand. At our greenfield manufacturing complex, capacity addition will commence with wooden pencils, followed by writing instruments, pencil accessories including erasers and sharpeners, and subsequently scholastic art materials. This phased approach enables efficient capital deployment and asset utilisation while providing the flexibility to respond to changing market dynamics and consumer demand. 



#### **Quality at Every Stage** 

Quality defines every stage of our manufacturing operations. Rigorous standards guide raw material sourcing, processing, production, packaging and distribution. Dedicated quality assurance and quality control teams uphold robust quality management systems through standard operating procedures and comprehensive testing protocols. 

Our integrated manufacturing model and expanding in-house capabilities reduce reliance on third-party developers. Greater control over production processes enhances operational efficiency and ensures consistent product quality across all manufacturing facilities.

#### **Data and Analytics** 

Our Sales Force Automation (SFA) platform enables our sales teams to capture customer demand, real time orders and retail insights, supporting informed sales planning and strategy development. The platform streamlines order management, strengthens customer engagement and enhances coordination across the distribution network, enabling faster execution and improved demand fulfilment. 

Our Distributor Management System (DMS) streamlines coordination across our distributor network by facilitating efficient order management, improving inventory visibility and supporting timely stock replenishment. The platform strengthens pricing compliance, enhances supply chain visibility and enables effective performance tracking across channels. These capabilities improve decision making, optimise resource utilisation and deliver responsive service to our distributors and consumers. 

We harness digital intelligence to strengthen planning and operational performance. Actionable insights optimise sourcing, manufacturing and distribution decisions. Data-driven execution improves productivity and resource utilisation. These capabilities prepare us to scale confidently and pursue every ambition. 

#### **Technology Enabled Planning** 

Integrated digital platforms strengthen planning, inventory visibility and execution across our value chain. SAP, Distributor Management System (DMS) and Sales Force Automation (SFA) deliver real-time insights for informed decisions. These capabilities strengthen demand planning, inventory optimisation and production scheduling. They also enhance distributor coordination through seamless digital integration. Together, they improve responsiveness, operational efficiency and execution, preparing us to achieve every ambition.

## **Supply Chain** 

# Creating Flow Through Excellence 

Our integrated supply chain supports uninterrupted operations by combining strategic sourcing, manufacturing capabilities and an extensive distribution network. As the business expands, we are strengthening supplier partnerships, enhancing supply visibility and improving operational efficiencies to ensure products reach customers reliably and on time. A disciplined sourcing approach, supported by backward integration for selected inputs, multiple sourcing for critical raw materials and disciplined planning, enables us to respond effectively to changing market conditions while maintaining consistent quality and service standards. 

### **Distribution Network** 

Our multi-channel distribution network strengthens market reach across diverse sales channels. It spans general trade, modern trade, e-commerce, and exports. We continue strengthening our stationery and art materials network with focused investments. Simultaneously, we are expanding our baby hygiene distribution ecosystem. This approach enhances market access and elevates service excellence across every channel. Every expansion prepares us to fulfil greater ambitions with confidence.

## **Strategic Acquisitions** 

# Growing Our Portfolio. Strengthening Our Capabilities. 

Our strategic acquisitions are guided by a clear long-term intent and disciplined preparation. They strengthen our presence across adjacent product categories with discipline, enhance manufacturing capabilities, distribution reach and consumer access. During the year, we integrated these businesses into the DOMS ecosystem. We leveraged operational strengths, brand equity, and distribution networks. This enables us to unlock long-term value and scalable synergies. These partnerships expand our addressable market, portfolio diversification and advance our vision of building a diversified kids-centric consumer products portfolio. 

### **Expanding Manufacturing Capabilities in Paper Stationery** 

The acquisition of Super Treads Private Limited (STPL) has strengthened our paper stationery ecosystem by expanding manufacturing capabilities and improving our ability to serve customers across Eastern India. The facility enhances production 

capacity while bringing manufacturing closer to key markets, enabling faster deliveries and improved responsiveness. 

capabilities, improving operational efficiency and expanding our presence in the paper stationery segment, while creating a stronger platform for long-term growth. 

This integration supports our strategy of strengthening regional manufacturing 

### **Increasing Strategic Investment in Pioneer Stationery Private Limited** 

As part of our portfolio expansion strategy, we increased our stake in Pioneer Stationery Private Limited from 51% to 64%, reinforcing our presence in the paper stationery segment. Pioneer is engaged in the manufacturing, importing, exporting, trading, buying and selling of 

paper stationery products, complementing our existing portfolio and broadening our offerings across key product categories. 

ownership, we are better positioned to leverage Pioneer’s capabilities, strengthen our market presence and create greater value through an expanded product portfolio and enhanced customer reach. 

This strategic investment reflects our long-term perspective on the growing paper stationery market. By increasing our 



### **Expanding Our Portfolio with the Reynolds Brand** 

In June 2026, we signed an Asset Purchase Agreement to acquire the certain identified assets and with the iconic Reynolds brand from Reynolds Pens India Private Limited and its global affiliates. The acquisition includes intellectual property, business assets, employees, contracts and associated liabilities related to Reynolds’ pens, markers, highlighters and school supplies business. 

This strategic acquisition strengthens our portfolio across writing instruments and school supplies while expanding our presence in premium product segments. By leveraging Reynolds’ strong brand recognition and market reach, we are well positioned to deepen our presence across domestic and international markets. The transaction also includes a supply agreement for pen tips, supporting seamless operational integration and business continuity. 

## **Progress Across Existing Acquisitions** 

# Unlocking Shared Potential 

Our earlier strategic acquisitions continued to deliver value through deeper integration, capacity expansion and broader market access. During the year, we strengthened manufacturing capabilities, expanded product offerings and leveraged our distribution network to enhance operational synergies across these businesses. 

### **Strengthening our Presence in Baby Hygiene** 

The integration of Uniclan Healthcare Private Limited continued to gain momentum in financial year 2025-26. During the year, Uniclan commissioned its third diaper manufacturing line increasing annual production capacity to approximately 65 crore pieces. We also commenced operations of a new wet wipes production line adding annual capacity of approximately 1.72 crore packs. These investments strengthen our manufacturing capabilities and product portfolio. We continue to expand in  baby hygiene portfolio. Wet wipes complement our diaper portfolio as part of baby hygiene routine. This integrated offering creates greater opportunities and enhances consumer convenience. 

while building a dedicated hygiene platform. Existing DOMS channel partners are being onboarded to support the hygiene business with a focus on strengthening secondary sales, improving channel productivity and building a scalable distribution network across markets. 

By leveraging DOMS’ extensive distribution network, Uniclan is well positioned to expand its market reach. Over time, as Uniclan’s dedicated retail presence strengthens, it is expected to complement and further enhance the distribution of DOMS’ stationery and art material portfolio. 



We are progressively expanding Uniclan’s domestic distribution network by leveraging DOMS’ established channel ecosystem 

### **Expanding our School Bags Portfolio** 

Financial Year 2025-26 marked an important phase in the integration of Skido Industries Private Limited. The business delivered a strong performance during the year, driven by the successful Back to School launch of our expanded DOMS branded school bags range, building on the product and market insights gained during the pilot year in financial year 2025-26. While the business continues to progress through its scaling phase, the acquisition has strengthened our presence in the school bags segment and significantly expanded our addressable market under the DOMS brand. 



The integration has also delivered operational efficiencies through backward integration for school bags used in DOMS kits and combo packs. Backed by DOMS’ brand strength, product development capabilities and extensive distribution network, Skido is well positioned to broaden its portfolio, deepen its market presence and support our long term growth ambitions.

## **Community Welfare** 

# Creating Lasting Value Beyond Business 

We believe sustainable growth creates lasting value beyond business. Guided by our Corporate Social Responsibility Policy, we invest in meaningful community development. Oversight from the CSR Committee strengthens governance and accountability. Our initiatives align with the Companies Act, 2013. They address community priorities through a long-term and impact-driven approach. Education, healthcare, women empowerment, environmental sustainability and rural development remain our key focus areas. 

### **Our Approach** 

### **Strengthening Healthcare Infrastructure** 

Our CSR strategy promotes inclusive and sustainable development. We support initiatives that expand educational opportunities, strengthen healthcare access, improve quality of life and empowers Community. Projects are selected through careful needs assessments, Implementation partners ensure responsible execution and measurable outcomes. 

Healthcare remained a strategic focus throughout the year. We supported the development of a state-of-the-art hospital in Umbergaon, Gujarat. The initiative is being implemented through the Umargam Industries Association. Once operational, the hospital will improve access to quality healthcare. It will reduce dependence on distant medical facilities. The project is expected to strengthen regional healthcare infrastructure. Better access will support healthier communities and long-term regional development. 



Education and Skill Development Healthcare **Key** Women **Focus** Empowerment **Areas** Rural Development Environmental Sustainability 





### **Strengthening Communities Through Meaningful Action** 

During the year, we expanded community initiatives across our key operating locations. Education and women empowerment remained central priorities. Programmes supported learning opportunities, special education and vocational skill development. These initiatives strengthened pathways to employment and economic participation. 

### **Building Long Term Social Impact** 

The achievements from financial year 2025-26 marked an important milestone with the incorporation of DOMS Foundation. The Section 8 company provides a dedicated platform for CSR implementation. It strengthens governance and expands the long-term impact of our community initiatives. 

We also supported rural development and social inclusion initiatives. Support for mass marriage programmes advanced women empowerment and community well-being. Together, these efforts strengthened inclusive and sustainable development. 

We also identified two transformative ongoing projects in Umbergaon. These projects focus on establishing an educational institution and a hospital. Together, they will expand access to education and healthcare. They also reflect our commitment to preparing communities for a stronger future. 

## **Environment, Health and Safety** 

# Safe Today, Stronger Tomorrow 

Responsible operations begin with thoughtful preparation. Our Environment, Health and Safety framework integrates environmental stewardship, employee well-being and workplace safety. It combines responsible resource management with operational excellence and strong governance. This integrated approach strengthens resilience and creates lasting value for our stakeholders. 

### **Environmental Stewardship** 

### **Integrated Management Systems** 

#### **Energy and Emissions** 

Our integrated management systems establish consistent standards across quality, environmental and safety performance. These frameworks strengthen governance through disciplined processes and effective risk management. Consistent execution enhances operational efficiency and organisational accountability. 

Improving energy efficiency remains a strategic operational priority. We continue optimising energy consumption through efficient processes and sustainable infrastructure. Greater adoption of renewable energy supports cleaner manufacturing operations. These initiatives reduce greenhouse gas emissions and strengthen long-term operational resilience. 

Current certifications reflect our commitment to continuous improvement and regulatory compliance. By embedding these systems into everyday operations, we reinforce responsible growth and long-term preparedness.

## **Board of Directors** 

# Guiding Visionaries 



**Massimo Candela*** Chairperson and Non-Executive Director 

Massimo Candela, aged 61 years, is the Non-Executive Director of our Company. He holds a degree in Business Administration with major in corporate finance  from Bocconi University, Milan. He has been associated with F.I.L.A. since 1992 as chief executive officer. 



**Chandni Somaiya** Whole-time Director 

Chandni Somaiya, aged 53 years, is the Whole- time Director and one of the Individual Promoters of our Company.  She joined S-Tech Industries as a partner in 2004.  She is primarily involved in the manufacturing operations of the scholastic stationery division and general administrative operations at the Umbergaon manufacturing facilities. 



**Santosh Raveshia** Managing Director 

Santosh Raveshia, aged 50 years, is the Managing Director and one of the Individual Promoters of our Company. He has been associated with our Company since its incorporation as a Director and Promoter.  He joined R.R. Industries in 2000 and has been its partner since 2002. Further, he has been the partner of S-Tech Industries since 2006. He is primarily involved in all aspects of the business of our Company, including new product development, crafting, and executing go-to market strategies. 



**Sanjay Rajani** Whole-time Director 

Sanjay Rajani, aged 57 years, is the Whole- time Director and one of the Individual Promoters of our Company. He joined R. R. Industries in 1985 and has been its partner since 2011. He is primarily involved in the production and product development of scholastic art and fine art materials in our Company. 



**Ketan Rajani** Whole-time Director 

Ketan Rajani, aged 55 years, is the Whole- time Director and one of the Individual Promoters of our Company.  He joined R. R. Industries in 2003 and has been its partner since 2011. **Om Raveshia#**

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company’s Standalone revenue from operations has increased by 19.92% to ₹ 2,04,964.07 lakhs as compared to ₹ 1,70,910.96 lakhs in the previous financial year. Domestic sales (net) has increased by 20.83% to ₹ 1,79,568.32 lakhs as compared to ₹ 1,48,608.97 lakhs in the previous financial year. Export sales (net) has increased by 13.79% to ₹ 25,048.85 lakhs as compared to ₹ 22,012.76 lakhs in the previous financial year.

## **DIVIDEND** 

The Board at its meeting held on May 18, 2026, recommended a final dividend of ₹ 3.65 per Equity Share of ₹ 10 each fully paid up for the financial year 2025-26 (previous financial year a final dividend of ₹ 3.15 per Equity Share was paid to the Shareholders). The payment of the final dividend is subject to approval of the Shareholders at the ensuing Annual General Meeting of the Company (‘AGM’), which is proposed to be convened on Thursday, September 03, 2026. 

The Company has fixed Thursday, August 27, 2026, as the record date for the purpose of determining the entitlement of Shareholders to receive the final dividend for the financial year 2025-26. The final dividend shall be paid on or before Friday, October 02, 2026. 

The dividend recommended is in accordance with the Company’s Dividend Distribution Policy. Pursuant to Regulation 43A of Securities 

and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (‘SEBI LODR Regulations’), as amended, the Company has adopted a Dividend Distribution Policy, which lays down the guiding principles for the Board and the management in relation to the declaration and distribution of dividend, with a view to ensuring fairness, transparency, sustainability and consistency in profit distribution to the shareholders. 

The Dividend Distribution Policy of the Company can be accessed on <u>https://domsindia.com/policies/.</u> 

# **TRASNFER TO RESERVES** 

For the financial year 2025-26, the Board of Directors have decided to retain the entire amount of profit in Statement of Profit & Loss as on March 31, 2026.

# **SIGNIFICANT/ MATERIAL EVENTS OCCURRED DURING THE FINANCIAL YEAR** 

## **a. Acquisition of Equity Stake** 

- i. On June 01, 2025, the Company acquired 51.00% of Equity Share capital in Super Treads Private Limited (‘STPL’) and effective from that date, STPL became a Subsidiary of the Company. STPL is engaged in the business of manufacturing and marketing of all types of Paper Stationery products including notebooks, drawing books, etc. This strategic acquisition enables the Company to continue expanding its presence in the Paper Stationery Industry. It enhances the Company’s manufacturing capabilities and capacities in the segment and strengthens its ability to efficiently cater to the paper stationery requirements of the Eastern markets of India. 

- ii Pursuant to the approval of the Board, the Company acquired an additional 13.00% Equity Share capital in Pioneer Stationery Private Limited (‘Pioneer’), an unlisted material subsidiary of the Company. The said acquisition was completed in two tranches, with 6.50% Equity Shares being acquired on August 30, 2025 and the remaining 6.50% Equity Shares was acquired on March 31, 2026. 

Consequent to the aforesaid acquisitions, the Company’s shareholding in Pioneer increased to 64.00% of the total paid-up Equity Share capital of Pioneer. 

## **b. Incorporation of DOMS Foundation** 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company along with its Subsidiaries and Associate Company incorporated a new company in the name of ‘DOMS Foundation’ under Section 8 of The Companies Act, 2013 (‘the Act’), a company limited by guarantee not having share capital. DOMS Foundation has been incorporated to undertake the Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) activities, donation, other charitable and not-for-profit activities in accordance with its objects and applicable laws. 

## **c. Formation of Joint Venture with Seven S.p.A** 

The Board at its meeting held on January 30, 2026, approved the proposal for formation of a 50:50 Joint Venture Company (‘JVC’) in India with Seven SpA (‘Seven’), a Group Company of F.I.L.A. - Fabbrica Italiana Lapis Ed Affini S.p.A., to focus on backpacks, pencil cases and bags. The proposed JVC is intended to leverage the collective industry and manufacturing capabilities of the joint venture partners for the supply of products for the benefit of the F.I.L.A. Group globally as well as for development of a differentiated range for the Indian market. The Company is in the process of incorporating the JVC, which will be incorporated upon fulfillment of the applicable statutory and regulatory requirements. 

# **MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSIS REPORT** 

The detailed operational performance of your Company has been comprehensively disclosed in the Management Discussion and Analysis Report which forms an integral part of this Annual Report. 

# **MATERIAL CHANGES AND COMMITMENTS OCCURRED AFTER THE CLOSE OF THE FINANCIAL YEAR TILL THE DATE OF THIS REPORT AFFECTING FINANCIAL POSITION OF THE COMPANY** 

Post March 31, 2026 and pursuant to the approval of the Board, the Company entered into an Asset Purchase Agreement with Reynolds Pens India Private Limited; Sanford, L.P.; Luxembourg Brands S.à r.l., Newell Europe S.à r.l.; NWL Valence Services S.A.S.; and NWL Switzerland S.à r.l., for the acquisition of certain assets, relevant contracts, employees, intellectual property and associated identified liabilities relating to the manufacture and sale of pens, markers, highlighters and school supplies under the Reynolds brand. The aggregate consideration for the acquisition is US$ 3.7 million, excluding the value of inventories. The said Asset Purchase transaction was completed on July 01, 2026. 

Except as stated above, no material changes or commitments affecting the financial position of the Company have occurred after the close of the financial year till the date of this Report. 

# **SHARE CAPITAL** 

There was no change in the Authorised Share Capital of the Company during the financial year 2025-26. However, there were changes in the Issued, Subscribed and Paid-up Share Capital of the Company, as detailed below: 

## **Authorised Share Capital** 

As on March 31, 2026, the Authorised Equity Share capital of the Company was ₹ 7,000.00 lakhs comprising of 7,00,00,000 Equity Shares of face value of ₹ 10 each. 

## **Issued, Subscribed and Paid-up Share Capital** 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company allotted 1,100 Equity Shares of face value ₹ 10 each pursuant to the exercise of options granted under the Company’s Employee Stock Option Plan 2023. Consequent to the aforesaid allotment, the issued, subscribed and paid-up Equity Share capital of the Company increased to ₹ 6,068.83 lakhs comprising of 6,06,88,336 Equity Shares of face value of ₹ 10 each from ₹ 6,068.72 lakhs comprising of 6,06,87,236 Equity Shares of face value of ₹ 10 each. 

As on March 31, 2026, the issued, Subscribed and Paid-up Equity Share capital of the Company was increased to ₹ 6,068.83 lakhs comprising of 6,06,88,336 Equity Shares of face value of ₹ 10 each. 

# **EMPLOYEE STOCK OPTION PLAN 2023** 

The Company has adopted DOMS Industries Limited Employee Stock Option Plan 2023 (‘ESOP 2023’/ ‘the Plan’). The Plan was formulated with an aim to attract, retain and motivate key talents working with the Company by way of rewarding their performance, to encourage them to contribute to the overall corporate growth and profitability and to promote employee loyalty to the Company. 

The Company confirms that the Plan is in compliance with the applicable provisions of The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Share Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) Regulations, 2021 (‘SEBI SBEB Regulations’) and there have been no changes to the Plan. 

# **CHANGE IN NATURE OF BUSINESS, IF ANY** 

There has been no change in the nature of business of the Company during the financial year 2025-26. 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company granted additional 1,37,690 stock options to certain eligible employees of the Company

# **DIRECTORS AND KEY MANAGERIAL PERSONNEL** 

The composition of the Board of Directors of the Company is in compliance with the provisions of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations. As on March 31, 2026, the Board comprised of 16 (sixteen) Directors, consisting of 01 (one) Managing Director, 04 (four) Whole-time Directors, 03 (three) Non-Executive Non-Independent Directors and 08 (eight) Non-Executive Independent Directors. The Board includes 03 (three) Women Directors including 01 (one) Woman Independent Director. 

The details relating to the composition of the Board and its Committees, tenure of Directors, areas of expertise and other relevant information are provided in the Corporate Governance Report, which forms part of this Annual Report. 

## **a. Changes in Directors and Key Managerial Personnel** 

- During the financial year 2025-26, the composition of the Board of Directors and Key Managerial Personnel of the Company underwent the following changes: 

### **i. Cessation of Director** 

- Cristian Nicoletti (DIN: 10042858), resigned as a NonExecutive Non-Independent Director of the Company with effect from the close of business hours on May 13, 2025. 

### **ii Appointment of Directors** 

Based on the recommendation of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and subject to approval of the Shareholders of the Company, the Board at its meeting held on May 19, 2025, approved the appointment of the following individuals as Directors on the Board of the Company. 

1. Om Raveshia (DIN: 09618267) as Whole-time Director of the Company for a term of 05 (five) years commencing from May 19, 2025 to May 18, 2030; and 

2. Piyush Mehta (DIN: 02380540), Harsh Thakkar (DIN: 11098669), Rohan Ghalla (DIN: 03210524) and Nitesh Shah (DIN: 11065275), as Non-Executive 

Independent Directors of the Company for a term of 05 (five) consecutive years commencing from May 19, 2025 to May 18, 2030. 

The said appointments were subsequently approved by the Shareholders of the Company through Postal Ballot on August 14, 2025. 

### **iii. Change in Chairperson** 

During the financial year 2025-26, effective from May 19, 2025, Gianmatteo Terruzi ceased to be the Chairperson of the Company. Further, effective from May 20, 2025, Massimo Candela was appointed as the Chairperson of the Company. 

## **b. Changes in Directors Post March 31, 2026** 

Based on the recommendation of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and subject to approval of the Shareholders of the Company, the Board at its meeting held on May 18, 2026, approved the re-appointment of Santosh Raveshia (DIN: 00147624) as Managing Director and Sanjay Rajani (DIN: 03329095) as Whole-time Director of the Company for a further period of 05 (five) years each, commencing from January 01, 2027 upto December 31, 2031, both days inclusive. Further, pursuant to the provisions of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations, approval of the Shareholders was required for their re-appointment. Accordingly, the Board at its meeting held on June 10, 2026, approved the Postal Ballot Notice with the Explanatory Statement seeking the approval of the Shareholders for the aforesaid re-appointments. The Postal Ballot Notice was dispatched to the Shareholders on June 15, 2026. The Shareholders of the Company approved and ratified the reappointment of Santosh Raveshia and Sanjay Rajani through Postal Ballot on July 15, 2026. 

## **c. Directors Liable to retire by rotation** 

In accordance with the provisions of Section 152 and other applicable provisions, if any, of the Act and the Articles of Association of the Company, Massimo Candela (DIN: 05189114) and Luca Pelosin (DIN: 05189104) Non-Executive NonIndependent Directors of the Company being the Directors longest in office since their last appointment, shall be liable to retire by rotation at the ensuing AGM and being eligible, offer themselves for re-appointment. The Board of Directors, based on the recommendation of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee (‘NRC’) have recommended their re-appointment for the approval of the Shareholders at the ensuing AGM. 

The requisite details of the Directors seeking reappointment, pursuant to Regulation 36(3) of SEBI LODR Regulations and Secretarial Standards on General Meetings (SS-2) are provided in the Notice of the 20<sup>th</sup> Annual General Meeting of the Company, proposed to be held on Thursday, September 03, 2026.

# **COMMITTEES TO THE BOARD** 

As on March 31, 2026, the Board has 05 (five) Committees as stated follows: 

1. Audit Committee 

2. Nomination and Remuneration Committee 

3. Stakeholders Relationship Committee 

4. Corporate Social Responsibility Committee 

5. Risk Management Committee 

The Committees play an important role in the governance framework of the Company and are entrusted with the responsibility to review and oversee specific matters requiring focused attention and operate in accordance with their respective terms of reference, as approved by the Board. 

During the financial year 2025-26, all recommendations made by the Committees were approved by the Board. 

The composition of the Committees and the details of the Committee meetings held during the financial year 2025-26 are provided in the Corporate Governance Report, which forms part of this Annual Report. 

# **CREDIT RATINGS AND REVISIONS THERETO** 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company’s credit rating on the Bank Loan facilities was upgraded from ‘CRISIL A+/Positive’ to ‘CRISIL AA-/Stable’. 

The information on credit ratings is available on the Company’s website at <u>https://domsindia.com/pdf/Investor_Relations/LODR/ Crisil_Credit_Ratings_Letter.pdf.</u> 

# **INVESTOR EDUCATION AND PROTECTION FUND** 

Your Company is not required to transfer any amount of unpaid/ unclaimed dividend for the financial year 2025-26 to the Investor Education and Protection Fund (‘IEPF’). 

# **POLICY ON DIRECTOR’S APPOINTMENT AND REMUNERATION** 

In accordance with the provisions of Section 178(3) of the Act and based on the recommendation of the Nomination & Remuneration Committee of the Company, the Board has approved a Nomination and Remuneration Policy which lays down the criteria for identification, selection, appointment and remuneration of Directors, Key Managerial Personnel and Senior Management Personnel. It also determines qualifications, positive attributes, independence of director and such related matters. 

The Nomination and Remuneration Policy is available on the Company’s website at <u>https://domsindia.com/policies/.</u> 

# **BOARD EVALUATION** 

In accordance with the provisions of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations it is required to evaluate the performance of: 

- (i) the Board as a whole; 

- (ii) the Individual Directors (including Independent Directors and Chairperson) ; and 

- (iii)  the committees of the Board. 

The Board established a formal mechanism to carry out an annual evaluation of its own performance, the performance of the Committees, Individual Directors and Chairperson pursuant to requirements of the provisions of Section 178 of the Act, Schedule IV and Regulation 17(10) of the SEBI LODR Regulations. The key objectives of the Board Evaluation process is to ensure that the Board and its Committees have appropriate composition and function collectively to achieve the business objectives of the Company. 

Similarly, the key objectives of conducting performance evaluation of the Directors through individual assessment and peer assessment were to ascertain if the Directors actively participate in the Board/ 

Annual Report **2025-26 47** 

Committee Meetings and contribute to achieve the common business goals of the Company. The Performance evaluation of the Board of Directors, Committees to the Board was done based on the structured questionnaire taking into consideration of various performance related aspects to ensure comprehensive assessment. 

The evaluation process was conducted through internal assessments and defined evaluation parameters. The details of the process are provided in the Corporate Governance Report, which forms part of this Annual Report. 

# **DISCLOSURE OF REMUNERATION TO DIRECTORS AND EMPLOYEES** 

In accordance with the provisions of Section 197(12) of the Act read with Rules 5(2) and 5(3) of The Companies (Appointment and Remuneration of Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014, a statement showing the names of the top ten employees in terms of remuneration drawn, along with the names and other particulars of the employees drawing remuneration in excess of the limits prescribed under the said rules, forms part of this Report. 

Further, disclosures relating to remuneration and other details as required under Section 197(12) of the Act read with Rule 5(1) of the Companies (Appointment and Remuneration of Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014, are provided in ‘Annexure I’ which forms part of this Report. 

Having regard to the provisions of the second proviso to Section 136(1) of the Act and as advised, the Annual Report excluding the aforesaid information is being sent to the Shareholders of the Company. Any shareholder interested in obtaining such information may address their email to ir@domsindia.com. 

# **DIRECTORS RESPONSIBILITY STATEMENT** 

Pursuant to the provisions of Section 134(3)(c) of the Act, your Directors, to the best of their knowledge and belief, hereby confirm that: 

- a. in the preparation of the annual accounts for the financial year ended March 31, 2026, the applicable accounting standards have been followed with proper explanation relating to material departures, if any; 

- b. they have selected such accounting policies and applied them consistently and made judgments and estimates that are reasonable and prudent so as to give a true and fair view of the state of affairs of the Company as at March 31, 2026 and the profit of the Company as at March 31, 2026; 

- c. they have taken proper and sufficient care for the maintenance of adequate accounting records in accordance with the provisions of this Act for safeguarding the assets of the Company and for preventing and detecting fraud and other irregularities; 

- d. they have prepared the annual accounts on a going concern basis; 

- e. they have laid down internal financial controls to be followed by the company and that such internal financial controls are adequate and operating effectively; and 

- f. the Directors have devised proper systems to ensure compliance with the provisions of all applicable laws and that such systems are adequate and operating effectively. 

# **INTERNAL FINANCIAL CONTROLS AND THEIR ADEQUACY** 

The Company has in place adequate Internal Financial Controls with reference to the financial statements. The Company’s internal financial controls and systems are adequate commensurate with the nature and size of the Company and it ensures compliance with the policies and procedures adopted by the Company for ensuring the orderly and efficient conduct of its business, including adherence to its policies, the safeguarding of its assets, the prevention and detection of frauds and errors, the accuracy and completeness of the accounting records and the timely preparation of reliable financial information. 

The Company’s internal control procedures which include internal financial controls, ensure compliance with various policies, practices and statutes and keeping in view the organisations pace of growth and increasing complexity of operations. This ensures safeguarding of assets and properties of the Company and protects against unauthorized use and disposal of the assets. 

The Audit Committee periodically reviews the adequacy and effectiveness of internal control systems and provides guidance for continuous improvement and strengthening. 

# **DETAILS OF SUBSIDIARY, JOINT VENTURES AND ASSOCIATE COMPANY** 

As on March 31, 2026, the Company has 06 (six) Subsidiaries and 01 (one) Associate Company. The details regarding the disclosure with respect to Subsidiaries and Associate Company of the Company in Form AOC-1 are provided in ‘Annexure II’, which forms part of this Report. 

## **Performance of the Subsidiary Company** 

### **1. Pioneer Stationery Private Limited** 

- Pioneer Stationery Private Limited (‘Pioneer’) is an unlisted material Subsidiary of the Company and is engaged in the business of manufacturing, importing, exporting, trading, buying and selling of Paper Stationery products. The management of the Company is focused on enhancing quality, efficiency and effectiveness of the business to achieve best-in-class performance. The Board of Directors of Pioneer frequently reviews its performance to ensure alignment with the Company’s strategic goals. 

During financial year 2025-26, revenue from operations of Pioneer marginally decreased by 1.10% to ₹ 16,862.72 lakhs as compared to ₹ 17,050.48 lakhs in the previous financial year. It’s Operating Profit for the financial year 2025-26 decreased by 22.34% to ₹ 1,769.77 lakhs as compared to ₹ 2,278.75 lakhs in previous financial year. Net Profit After Tax of Pioneer decreased by 19.36% to ₹ 1,186.46 lakhs as compared to ₹ 1,471.29 lakhs in previous financial year.

### **2. Micro Wood Private Limited** 

Micro Wood Private Limited (‘Micro Wood’) is a Subsidiary of the Company and is engaged in the business of manufacturing Tin and Paper-based packing materials. The Board of Directors of Micro Wood frequently review its performance to ensure continued growth and alignment with the Company’s objective. 

During financial year 2025-26, revenue from operations of Micro Wood has increased by 17.22% to ₹ 10,563.48 lakhs as compared to ₹ 9,011.68 lakhs in the previous financial year. It’s Operating Profit for the financial year 2025-26 marginally decreased by 0.17% to ₹ 1,407.19 lakhs as compared to ₹ 1,409.62 lakhs in the previous financial year. Net Profit After Tax of Micro Wood decreased by 19.21% to ₹ 458.50 lakhs as compared to ₹ 567.50 lakhs in previous financial year. 

### **3. Skido Industries Private Limited** 

Skido Industries Private Limited (‘Skido’) is a Subsidiary of the Company and is engaged in the business of designing, manufacturing, marketing and selling of all types of Bags, Pouches and other related products. The Board of Directors of Skido frequently review its performance to ensure continued growth and alignment with the Company’s objective. 

During the financial year 2025-26, revenue from operations of Skido has increased by 57.21% to ₹ 1,408.02 lakhs as compared to ₹ 895.64 lakhs in the previous financial year. It’s Operating Profit for the financial year 2025-26 has increased by 98.23% to ₹ 189.96 lakhs as compared to ₹ 95.83 lakhs in the previous financial year. Net Profit After Tax of Skido has increased by 82.18% to ₹ 121.13 lakhs as compared to ₹ 66.49 lakhs in the previous financial year. 

### **6. DOMS Foundation** 

DOMS Foundation became a Subsidiary of the Company with effect from March 19, 2026. DOMS Foundation has been incorporated under Section 8 of the Act, to promote, undertake, plan, organize, implement and execute various Corporate Social Responsibility activities, donation, other charitable and not-for-profit activities in accordance with its objects and applicable laws. Further, there have been no transactions in DOMS Foundation from the date of its incorporation up to the reporting date. Accordingly, the same has no impact on the consolidated financials of the Company. 

Accordingly, the Audited Financial Statements of all the Subsidiaries except DOMS Foundation are available on the website of the - Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/financial statement-of-subsidiariesgroup-companies/.</u>

# **CORPORATE SOCIAL RESPONSIBILITY (‘CSR’)** 

DOMS is committed to conducting its business in a socially responsible manner and actively contributes towards the sustainable development of the society. The Company’s CSR initiatives focus, inter alia, on healthcare, education, empowerment of women, rural development, affordable housing, disaster relief, welfare of armed forces, socio-economic development and the upliftment and welfare of the Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes, other backward classes, minorities, and other areas of public service, as prescribed under Schedule VII of the Act. 

The CSR Committee confirms that the implementation and monitoring of the CSR activities was done in compliance with the CSR Policy, objectives and in accordance with the provisions of the Act. The CSR Policy is available at the website of the Company and can be accessed at https://domsindia.com/policies/. 

In accordance with Section 135 of the Act and The Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014, read with relevant clarifications issued by Ministry of Corporate Affairs, the Company has undertaken activities as per the Company’s CSR Policy and the necessary disclosure on CSR activities are provided in ‘Annexure III’ which forms part of this Report.

# **RISK MANAGEMENT** 

The Company recognizes that risk is an integral part of business operations and is committed to managing risks in a proactive and efficient manner. The Company has formulated and implemented 

a robust Risk Management Policy to identify, assess, monitor and mitigate various internal and external risks. The objective of Risk Management Policy is to ensure sustainable business growth, operational stability and to promote a proactive approach towards reporting, evaluating and addressing risks associated with the Company’s business. 

The Risk Management Policy provides a structured and disciplined framework for risk management and assists the management and the Board in decision-making relating to risk-associated matters. The Risk Management Policy of the Company is available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/policies/.</u> 

In compliance with Regulation 21 of the SEBI LODR Regulations, the Board has constituted a Risk Management Committee, which is entrusted with the roles and responsibilities as specified under Part D of Schedule II of the SEBI LODR Regulations. The Committee periodically reviews the adequacy and effectiveness of the Company’s risk management systems, taking into account the evolving regulatory environment, changing macro-economic conditions and business complexities. 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company identified and assessed the internal and external risks associated with its business operations and implemented appropriate mitigation to address such risks. The identified risks were periodically reviewed to ensure effective monitoring and mitigation. A detailed analysis of internal and external risks is provided in the Management Discussion and Analysis Report which forms part of this report. 

# **VIGIL MECHANISM** 

The Company is committed to conducting its business operations in ethical, responsible and transparent manner and upholds the highest standards of corporate governance. To foster a culture of accountability and transparency, the Company continuously reviews its existing policies, systems and procedures, ensuring they remain aligned with evolving governance practices and regulatory expectations. 

The Company has established a robust vigil mechanism and adopted a Whistle Blower and Vigil Mechanism Policy, duly approved by the Board of Directors pursuant to the requirements of Section 177(9) of the Act read with Rule 7 of the Companies (Meetings of Board and its Powers) Rules, 2014 and Regulation 22 of SEBI LODR Regulations. 

The Whistle Blower and Vigil Mechanism Policy provides a secure framework for Directors, employees and other stakeholders to report genuine concerns about unethical behaviour, suspected fraud or violation of the Company’s code of conduct or policies. The policy ensures adequate safeguards against victimization of persons who avail of the mechanism. 

Further, during the financial year 2025-26, no individual was denied access to the Chairperson of the Audit Committee or any appropriate authority under the Vigil Mechanism. 

The Whistle Blower and Vigil Mechanism Policy of the Company is available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/policies/.</u>

# Annexure III 

# **Annual Report on Corporate Social Responsibility (‘CSR’)** 

#### **Pursuant to Section 135 of the Act read with Rule 8 of the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014** 

## **1. Brief outline on CSR Policy of the Company:** 

In line with its core values and continued commitment to quality, social welfare, sustainable development of communities and corporate compliance, the Company endeavours to address the needs of underprivileged communities, in a sensitive and impactful manner and enabling them to achieve a better quality of life. As a responsible corporate citizen, the Company contributes to inclusive growth by empowering communities and supporting socio-economic development. 

The main objective of the Company towards Corporate Social Responsibility is: 

- To ensure that it is committed to operate its business in an economically, socially and environmentally sustainable manner, while recognizing the interests of all its stakeholders. 

- To take up programmes that benefits the communities in and around its work centres and over a period, results in enhancing the quality of life of the people in the area of its business operations. 

- To generate a community goodwill for the Company and help reinforce a positive and socially responsible image of the Company as a good corporate citizen of the Country. 

The CSR activities undertaken by the Company are aligned with the requirements of Schedule VII of Section 135 of the Act read with The Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014 (including any statutory modification(s) or re-enactment(s) thereof as applicable from time to time.

### **CSR Initiatives taken during the financial year 2025-26** 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company directly and through implementing agencies, undertook CSR initiatives under the focus area of (i) Promoting education, including special education and employment enhancing vocation skills and (ii) Empowerment of Women as specified under Schedule VII of the Act, thereby contributing to the overall social welfare and community development. 

### **Incorporation of DOMS Foundation** 

The Company along with its Subsidiaries and Associate Company, incorporated DOMS Foundation under Section 8 of the Act, to promote, undertake, plan, organize, implement and execute various CSR activities, donation, other charitable and not-for-profit activities in accordance with its objects and applicable laws. 

### **Identification of Ongoing Projects** 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company has identified two ongoing projects, namely (i) Setting up of an educational institution in Umbergaon; and (ii) Setting up of hospital in Umbergaon.

# Annexure IV 

# **CONSERVATION OF ENERGY, TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION AND FOREIGN EXCHANGE EARNINGS AND OUTGO Pursuant to Section 134 of the Act read with The Companies (Accounts) Rules, 2014** 

## **A. Conservation of Energy** 

- The Company remains committed to energy conservation and the progressive adoption of alternative energy sources across its business operations. During the financial year, the Company continued systematic efforts to identify and implement energy-efficiency initiatives at its business premises. 

The key measures undertaken by the Company at its plants for conservation of energy are stated below: 

- **Renewable energy sources:** During the financial year, the Company generated, 3,510.61 GJ of solar energy from its 1MW Solar Plant installed at its Manufacturing facility located in Umbergaon, Gujarat. 

- **Wood Briquettes as a ‘fuel’:** The Company collects sufficient sawdust generated during the wooden pencil manufacturing process, comprising leftover wood shavings and lead and thereafter recycles it into wood briquettes at its onsite facility. These high calorific briquettes are used as an alternative fuel for furnace heating, substituting conventional fuels. During the financial year, the Company produced briquettes with an energy capacity of approximately 1.60 lakhs GJ, which helped the Company substitute other fuels for furnace heating. 

- **Reduction in Diesel Consumption:** The Company has installed 66KV High Tension Express Feeder Line at its flagship production plant in Umbergaon, Gujarat, thereby reducing the use of diesel for power generation. 

- **Use of Natural Lights and LED Lights:** The Company ensures adequate large windows at its manufacturing units to maximize the use of natural light. It is also installing LED lights across its manufacturing locations to reduce power consumption. 

These initiatives have contributed to a reduction in specific energy consumption and enhanced operational sustainability. The Company remains committed to undertaking further measures aimed at reducing energy intensity and increasing the use of renewable energy. 

## **B. Technical Absorption** 

- a. Our R&D efforts are focussed on product development, product improvement, cost reduction and integration of modern technologies to our processes, which help us in improving our operational efficiency. 

- b. The Company’s R&D facilities are equipped with modern and advanced equipment’s to develop, test and evaluate our products. 

- c. Further, new processes which are developed in our R&D units are initially implemented on a pilot basis at our Umbergaon manufacturing facilities to understand the efficacy and challenges before initiating commercial production. 

- d. The Company has consistently introduced new products, improved product quality and improved product user friendliness which has helped the Company in cost reduction and expanding its consumer base, thus increasing sales and profitability. 

## **C. Foreign Exchanges Earnings & Outgo** 

- a. The Company exports its products to over 55 countries globally in regions such as America, Europe, Middle East, Africa and Asia. 

- b. The Details of Foreign Exchange Earnings and Outgo are stated on Standalone basis as follows:
