# **Secretarial Audit Report** 

## **FORM NO. MR-3** 

## **FOR THE FINANCIAL YEAR ENDED MARCH 31, 2026** 

**Pursuant to Section 204(1) of The Companies Act, 2013 and Rule No. 9 of The Companies (Appointment and Remuneration of Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014** 

To, 

The Members, **DOMS Industries Limited,** J-19, G.I.D.C, Opp. Telephone Exchange, Umbergaon - 396171, Gujarat. 

I have conducted the Secretarial Audit for the compliance of applicable statutory provisions and the adherence to good corporate practices by DOMS Industries Limited (hereinafter referred to as ‘the Company’). The Secretarial Audit was conducted in a manner that provided me a reasonable basis for evaluating the corporate conducts/ statutory compliances and expressing my opinion thereon. 

Based on my verification of the Company’s books, papers, minute books, forms and returns filed, and other records, as well as the information provided by the Company, its officers, agents and authorized representatives during the conduct of Secretarial Audit, I hereby report that in my opinion, the Company has, during the audit period covering the financial year ended on March 31, 2026, (‘the Audit Period’) complied with the statutory provisions listed hereunder and also that the Company has proper Board processes and compliance mechanism in place to the extent, in the manner and subject to the reporting made hereinafter. 

I have examined the books, papers, minute books, forms and returns filed and other records maintained by the Company for the financial year ended on March 31, 2026, according to the provisions of: 

1. The Companies Act, 2013 (‘the Act’) and the rules made thereunder; 

2. The Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956 (‘SCRA’) and the rules made thereunder; 

3. The Depositories Act, 1996 and the Regulations and Bye-laws framed thereunder; 

4. Foreign Exchange Management Act, 1999 and the rules and regulations made thereunder to the extent, applicable of Foreign Direct Investment (Overseas Direct Investment and External Commercial Borrowings are not applicable to the Company); 

5. The following Regulations and Guidelines prescribed under the Securities and Exchange Board of India Act, 1992 (‘SEBI Act’): 

   - The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Substantial Acquisition of Shares and Takeovers) Regulations, 2011; 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations, 2015; 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2018; 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015; 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Share Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) Regulations, 2021; and 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Registrars to an Issue and Share Transfer Agents) Regulations, 1993 regarding the Act and dealing with client. 

As informed by the Management, there are no laws which specifically apply to the type of activities undertaken by the Company. 

I have also examined compliance with the applicable clauses of the following: 

1. Secretarial Standards with respect to Meetings of Board of Directors (SS-1) and General Meetings (SS-2) issued by The Institute of Company Secretaries of India. 

2. The Listing Agreements entered into by the Company with BSE Limited and National Stock Exchange of India Limited. 

I report that, during the Audit Period, the Company has complied with the provisions of the Act, Rules, Regulations, Guidelines, Standards, etc., as mentioned above. One e-form, MGT-6, was filed with additional fees. 

I further report that, during the Audit Period, the following Regulations and Guidelines prescribed under the Securities and Exchange Board of India Act, 1992 (‘SEBI Act’) were not applicable to the Company: 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Issue and Listing of Non-Convertible Securities) Regulations, 2021; 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Delisting of Equity Shares) Regulations, 2021; and 

- The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Buyback of Securities) Regulations, 2018. 

Annual Report **2025-26 59** 

# I further report that, 

The Board of Directors of the Company is duly constituted with proper balance of Executive Directors, Non-Executive Directors and Independent Directors. There were changes in the composition of the Board of Directors during the Audit Period. 

Adequate notice was given to all Directors to schedule the Meetings of the Board of Directors and other Board Committee Meetings, agenda and detailed notes on agenda were sent at least seven days in advance for meetings other than those held at shorter notice and a system exists for seeking and obtaining further information and clarifications on the agenda items before the meeting and for meaningful participation at the meeting. 

All Decisions taken at the Meetings of the Board of Directors and Board Committee Meetings were carried through on the basis of majority and were properly entered in the respective Minutes book. Dissenting views/suggestions, if any, by any member of the Board of Directors/Committee were properly recorded. 

I further report that, there are adequate systems and processes in the Company commensurate with the size and operations of the Company to monitor and ensure compliance with applicable laws, rules, regulations and guidelines. 

I further report that, during the Audit Period the Company had undertaken following specific events/ actions having a major bearing on the Company’s affairs in pursuance to the provisions of the Act, Rules, Regulations, Guidelines, Standards, etc., as mentioned above: 

1. The Company has made an equity investment in Super Treads Private Limited by way of acquiring 51.00% stake for an aggregate amount not exceeding ₹ 612 lakhs. 

2. The Company acquired additional equity stake in Pioneer Stationery Private Limited by increasing its shareholding from 51.00% to 64.00% of the total paid-up Equity Share capital. 

3. The Company granted 1,37,690 stock options to certain eligible employees of the Company and its Subsidiaries under DOMS Industries Limited Employee Stock Option Plan 2023. 

4. The Company along with its subsidiaries and associate company incorporated a new company under Section 8 of the Act, a company limited by guarantee not having share capital in the name of ‘DOMS Foundation’, to promote, undertake, plan, organize, implement and execute various CSR activities, donation, other charitable and not-for-profit activities in accordance with its objects and applicable laws. 

5. The Board approved the proposal for the formation of 50:50 Joint Venture in India with Seven S.p.A, a group company of F.I.L.A. - Fabbrica Italiana Lapis Ed Affini S.p.A.

# **Management Discussion and Analysis** 

## **Global Economy** 

The global economy remained resilient in calendar year 2025, with GDP expanding by 3.5% despite trade tensions and policy uncertainty. Technology-led investments, resilient consumer spending and favourable financial conditions supported growth. Inflationary pressures eased across most major economies during the year, as central banks maintained a cautious monetary stance. The gradual moderation in inflation enabled select economies to initiate calibrated policy easing, supporting investment sentiment and economic activity without undermining price stability. 

Geopolitical disruptions in the Middle East, particularly those affecting transit through the Strait of Hormuz, increased uncertainty in global trade flows contributed to supply chain disruption and higher raw material and freight costs. In response, companies accelerated efforts to diversify supply chains and strengthen regional manufacturing ecosystems, resulting in increased investment flows towards emerging manufacturing destinations, particularly across Asia. 

Looking ahead, the global economic outlook remains cautiously optimistic. Growth is expected to remain stable over the medium term, although risks persist in the form of geopolitical tensions, trade fragmentation, financial market volatility and structural imbalances in major economies. Policymakers are likely to prioritise macroeconomic stability, reinforce fiscal discipline and advance structural reforms aimed at enhancing productivity and supporting sustainable long-term growth. 

### **Overview of global GDP growth projections across advanced and emerging economies** 





















## **Indian Economy** 

Despite multiple challenges facing the global economy, including geopolitical fragmentation and increasing polarisation of power, the Indian economy remained one of the fastest-growing major 

economies in the world. The uncertain global environment disrupted international trade, affecting exports, supply chains, and the availability of raw materials. These factors contributed to higher prices and, consequently, influenced economic activity and the business environment worldwide. Amid these challenges, India recorded GDP growth of 7.7% in financial year 2025-26. This growth was supported by strong expansion in private consumption and fixed investment. From the supply side, the manufacturing and services sectors remained the key drivers of economic growth. 

Indian Government played a pivotal role in supporting the economic growth in India by undertaking initiatives aimed at strengthening domestic manufacturing and infrastructure development, including the Make in India and the Production Linked Incentive (PLI) scheme which continued to enhance manufacturing capabilities, promote export competitiveness and attract global supply chain investments. 

Inflationary pressures moderated during the year, contributing to a stable price environment.  Easing commodity prices and improved supply chain conditions further supported economic activity across sectors. Additionally, Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) inflows remained strong during the year, with both gross and net FDI showing improvement. India also continued to attract significant greenfield investments, reinforcing its position as a preferred investment destination. 

Looking ahead, India’s economic outlook remains favourable, supported by strong domestic demand, continued public investment and ongoing structural reforms. The proposed India–EU Free Trade Agreement, with a combined market of over INR 2091.6 Lakh Crore (USD 2.4 trillion), provides unprecedented access for over 99% of India’s exports. Sustained emphasis on infrastructure development, manufacturing expansion, digital transformation and supply chain diversification is expected to reinforce India’s position as a key global manufacturing and consumption hub.

# **Industry Review** 

## **Global Stationery Industry** 

The global stationery industry is expected to maintain its growth momentum over the coming year supported by sustained demand from educational institutions, workplaces and creative hobby segments. The global stationery industry market was valued at USD 165.87 billion in 2025 and is expected to grow from USD 173.93 billion in 2026 to USD 268.08 billion by 2034, exhibiting a CAGR of 5.56% during the forecast period of 2026-2034. 

From a regional standpoint, the Asia-Pacific region remained the largest market for stationery products, accounting for a significant share of global demand. The region benefits from large student population, expanding education systems and increasing literacy levels across emerging economies. Major markets, including China, India, and Japan, continued to remain central to regional consumption. During the reporting year, the Asia market made a significant contribution, supported by a rising number of student enrolments in schools and colleges across countries such as China, India, Bangladesh, and Vietnam. Among the developed economies, the United States remained one of the most significant markets globally. Demand for stationery as well as art and craft materials was supported by the growing interest in different forms of art and crafts. 

office supplies is increasing. These products are made from renewable resources, recycled and biodegradable materials, and are non-toxic and affordable. These factors have contributed to the growing global demand for eco-friendly stationery products. In addition to this, increased corporate activity continues to support demand for branded and customised stationery. In addition, the shift towards remote and hybrid working arrangements has contributed to higher consumption of home-office supplies. Overall, steady demand across education, corporate and creative segments is expected to support stable growth over the medium term.

# **Indian Stationery Market** 

The Indian stationery market has witnessed steady growth, supported by factors such as the rising literacy rate and increasing enrollment in schools and colleges. Together, these factors have supported demand for the stationery industry, particularly educational stationery products across the country. In addition, government initiatives focused on strengthening India's education sector have made a significant contribution to the growth of the stationery market. The India stationary market is projected to reach USD 18.08 billion by 2026.  Paper Products held the leading position among Product segments in 2025, based on market size<sup>8</sup> . 

Rapid growth of e-commerce and emerging quick-commerce platforms is expanding stationery accessibility across Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities.  In addition, favourable demographics continue to support longterm demand. With one of the largest student populations globally and a steadily expanding workforce, India remains a significant consumption market for stationery across educational institutions, workplaces and households. At the same time, intense competition among the established brands, along with emerging players, remains a significant challenge for the Indian stationery market. 

## **Key Trends Shaping Indian Stationery Industry** 

### **Urbanisation** 

Growing urbanisation and the expansion of smart cities are significantly increasing literacy rates and white-collar employment across India. This structural shift leads to a higher concentration of educational hubs and corporate offices in urban centers, which continuously generates stable demand for standard office supplies, writing instruments, and academic stationery. 

### **Product innovation** 

Growing consumer preference for eco-friendly and premium stationery products continued to drive product innovation. Manufacturers introducing sustainable product variants are differentiating their portfolios and capturing premium market segments. 

### **GST reform** 

The GST exemption on pencils, erasers, and exercise books aims to ease educational expenses by lowering the cost of basic learning materials. 

### **Expansion of institutional demand** 

Growth in educational institutions, coaching centres and corporate offices is sustaining demand for notebooks, writing instruments and office supplies. 

### **Premiumisation of stationery products** 

Rising disposable incomes and evolving consumer preferences are driving demand for branded and premium stationery, particularly among urban consumers and students. 

### **Growth of organised retail and e-commerce** 

### **Rise of personalised and creative stationery** 

Growing consumer interest in customised notebooks, planners and stationery kits, influenced by social media and lifestyle trends, is driving demand for premium and design-oriented products. 

The expansion of digital commerce platforms is improving accessibility across Tier II and Tier III cities, enabling brands to reach a wider consumer base. 

# **Growth Drivers** 

## **Rising School Enrolment and Government Education Spending** 

Rising school enrolment driven by India's young demographic and government initiatives such as NEP 2020 and the Right to Education Act is expanding the addressable student base. Increased per-student government spending on educational materials directly stimulates stationery demand across K-12 institutions 

## **Increased disposable income** 

Higher disposable income supported by tax exemptions, moderate inflation, and growth in economic activities, strengthened household spending, leading to increased demand for discretionary stationery products 

## **Rise of remote working culture** 

The shift towards remote and hybrid working expanded the demand for stationery beyond office environments and this increased the home-office usage of writing and organisational products 

## **Rising Demand for Premium and Personalised Stationery** 

Evolving consumer preferences and lifestyle trends are driving demand for premium, branded and customised stationery. Individuals and corporate clients are increasingly seeking personalised notebooks, planners and stationery kits. This shift is pushing manufacturers to actively diversify their sustainable product lines to stay competitive in the evolving market

## **Favourable government policy** 

### **• Manufacturing scheme** 

   - The Indian Government has continued to strengthen the manufacturing sector through initiatives such as Make in India and the Production-Linked Incentive (PLI) schemes. These programmes reduce import dependence, boost domestic production further supporting the domestic manufacturing of the stationery industry 

- **Pradhan Mantri Vidyalaxmi (PM-Vidyalaxmi) Scheme** 

   - Through the PM-Vidyalaxmi Scheme, the Indian Government aims to provide financial support to meritorious students for pursuing quality higher education, thereby offering collateralfree and guarantor-free education loans 

- **Focus on skill development** 

   - To support skill development and improve employability, the Indian Government introduced the SWAYAM portal, which is also transforming higher education which is contributing to demand for educational and stationery products 

### **School Bags Market** 

The global school bags market was valued at USD 26.2 billion in 2025 and will reach USD 41.3 billion by 2034 with a 5.02% CAGR<sup>9</sup> . Steady growth is driven by rising enrollments, design innovations, and eco-friendly recycled fibres. Asia Pacific leads globally, supported by functional advancements like ergonomic support and USB charging features. India generated USD 3.19 billion in 2025 accounting for 13.7% of the global market share. The domestic market  is expected to expand from USD 3.38 billion in 2026 to USD 5.56 billion in 2033 at a CAGR of 7.4%, due to rising enrollment rates and demand for durable, affordable options. Urban Indian buyers prefer branded, techenabled bags via organized retail, while rural consumers focus on durability through wholesale channels. Crucially, the Indian segment completely lacks credible, widely recognized brands. This highly fragmented landscape gives new players a massive penetration opportunity to build trust and capture substantial market share<sup>10</sup> . 

### **Diaper Market** 

The global diaper market reached a value of USD 97.2 billion in 2025 with Asia Pacific contributing 35.4% in revenue. The market is projected to reach USD 168.5 billion by 2034, exhibiting a CAGR of 6.30%<sup>11</sup> . This growth is driven by rising hygiene awareness, urbanization, online subscription models, and preferences for hypoallergenic products. The baby diaper segment accounts for 64.5% of this global demand. The Indian diaper market, valued at INR 138.2 Billion for financial year 2025 and projected to reach INR 251.6 billion by financial year 2030, is evolving from episodic use to routine hygiene consumption. The baby diaper segment dominates India with a 88.1% share from robust birth rates. Growth is fueled by rising middle-class incomes, aggressive retail expansion, and government initiatives like the Swachh Bharat Mission. 

### **• Government Procurement via GeM** 

   - Mandated by GFR Rule 149, the Government e-Marketplace (GeM) requires all central and state agencies to source stationery via the platform, establishing a high-volume, predictable revenue stream. This system ensures steady demand while prioritizing compliant domestic MSMEs through strict “Make in India” policies. 

- **State Government Initiatives** 

   - State level student welfare programs like the Telangana Education Kits Scheme and Andhra Pradesh’s automated student kit model mandate the universal distribution of free school bags, notebooks, and writing materials to millions of public school students annually. These aggressive distribution drives create massive, recurring bulk procurement contracts. This system ensures consistent year-round order volumes for regional stationery manufacturers. 

E-commerce expansion into Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities improves doorstep accessibility. Higher purchasing power and hygiene awareness shift diapers into everyday essentials. Technological advancements in absorbency and demand for biodegradable alternatives create new domestic opportunities<sup>12</sup> . 

# **Company Overview** 

DOMS Industries Limited is a kid-centric consumer company with an extensive portfolio of stationery, creative art materials, back to school products and baby hygiene products. The Company is one the India’s leading players in manufacturing and marketing of stationery and art material products. Its position is supported by a diversified product portfolio, robust brand equity, integrated manufacturing capabilities, and an extensive distribution network. The Company offers a comprehensive range of products across scholastic stationery, scholastic art materials, paper stationery, office supplies, hobby and craft products, fine art materials, back to school products, kits and combos and baby hygiene, with a portfolio comprising over 4700+ SKUs. The Company’s established portfolio of brands includes DOMS, C3, Amariz, Fixy Fix, Clapjoy, Wowper, and Reynolds. 

The Company’s growth is driven by sustained investments in product development, capacity augmentation and process enhancement across its manufacturing facilities. During the year, DOMS recorded healthy demand across several product categories, particularly Office Supplies, Kits and Combos, Hobby and Craft, Paper Stationery and Back to School, supported by new capacity additions, new product launches, and increased consumer engagement in creative and learning-oriented activities. The baby hygiene business also saw a significant uptick, fuelled by growing demand for diapers and enhanced capacity as compared to last year. DOMS

continues to expand its presence across domestic and international markets, supported by steady demand across stationery segments and growing export opportunities, despite prevailing global trade headwinds. The Company has a dedicated team working at its state-of-the-art R&D facility in Umbergaon, Gujarat, to develop, test and evaluate products. It is supported by an in-house designing team that develops concept-driven products based on market research and evolving consumer preferences. 

The Company continued to deliver sustainable growth by strengthening its market position through ongoing investments in capacity addition, product innovation and development, manufacturing, brand building and distribution expansion. Product innovation continues to remain a key driver of the Company’s growth, with an expanding portfolio across writing instruments, adhesives, scholastic stationery and adjacent categories supporting deeper market penetration through a network of over 130+ super stockists. Its strong distribution network, comprising more than 5,900 distribution partners, enables a timely response to market demand. The Company operates a widespread multi-channel distribution network across general trade, modern trade, leading e-commerce platforms and institutional sales. This supports effective market penetration across over 1,45,000 retail outlets. The extensive network is further supported by more than 1,000 sales personnel, strengthening market coverage and customer engagement. In addition, the Various IT enabled tools support coordination with the distributor network and sales team by managing orders, ensuring timely stock replenishment, and maintaining pricing compliance. These digital tools also improve decision-making, enhance supply chain visibility, and enable effective performance tracking across the Company’s extensive distribution network. Alongside product innovation, quality remains a key focus area for the organisation. The Company maintains stringent quality standards throughout every stage of the manufacturing cycle. Its in-house set-up and collaborative efforts have reduced dependence on third-party developers, resulting in improved efficiency and providing greater control over the overall process and product quality. DOMS is also expanding into adjacent child-centric segments with its earlier investment in Uniclan Healthcare, a manufacturer of baby diapers and wet wipes, 

as well as in Skido Industries, a manufacturer of school bags, pouches and related products, both of which have recorded healthy growth. In addition, DOMS has approved a 50:50 joint venture with Seven SpA, part of the F.I.L.A. Group ecosystem, to manufacture premium backpacks, bags and pencil cases. This collaboration brings together Seven’s design expertise with DOMS’ manufacturing capabilities. It is expected to support export growth while facilitating the introduction of premium back to school products in the domestic market. With continued emphasis on manufacturing, product development and brand building, DOMS remains well positioned to consolidate its leadership in the stationery and art materials sector while advancing adjacent growth segments. 

The Company remains focused on executing its long term growth strategy, with the commissioning of the 50+ acre expansion project being a key priority. The Company has taken possession of the first two buildings. Commercial production is expected to commence towards the end of second quarter of financial year 2026-27. This expansion is expected to enhance operational flexibility, strengthen manufacturing capabilities and support the Company’s next phase of growth. 

Alongside this, the Company is undertaking capital investments to expand capacity within its existing facilities and modernise key processes. These initiatives are expected to improve operational efficiency, enhance productivity and support growing customer demand. Backed by continued investments in manufacturing infrastructure and process improvements, the Company remains well positioned to strengthen its market presence, expand capacity and create sustainable long term value for its stakeholders. 

# **Key factors supporting the Company’s growth in Financial year 2026 as Growth Drivers** 

## **New product launches** 

New launches across product categories received strong consumer acceptance, supported by ergonomic and user-friendly designs. These launches comprised pencil boxes, school bags, pens, mechanical pencils, stamp pads, adhesives and differentiated SKUs across scholastic stationery, art, hobby and craft, as well as kits and combo packs 

### **Growth across product categories** 

The Company reported sustained growth across its product categories. Capacity additions supported stronger growth in certain categories, while the remaining categories recorded positive sales growth through enhancements in product offerings and higher average selling prices 

### **Strong domestic demand** 

The domestic market continued to remain buoyant and served as a key growth driver. This was supported by the Company's well-established distribution network, strong brand equity, and diversified product portfolio 

### **Steady export growth** 

Exports continued to register steady double-digit growth despite global uncertainties, including trade tensions, geopolitical conflicts, and regional instability. This reflected sustained demand across international markets 

### **Manufacturing infrastructure enhancements** 

The Company continued to strengthen its manufacturing capabilities through sustained capacity expansion and strategic investments. Capital expenditure during the

# **Scholastic Stationery** 

Scholastic Stationery comprises products used across schools, colleges, and universities. These items are also used by professionals, homes, and in general office environments. During the financial year 2025-26, this segment delivered a stable performance. This performance was supported by consistent demand from the education sector. During the year, the Company further strengthened its product portfolio with the introduction of mechanical pencils. However, the growth of the segment was primarily constrained due to capacity constraints. 

# **Office Supplies** 

Office supplies include essential products used in professional environment to support communication, organisation and day-to-day operations like pens, highlighters, markers, etc. During financial year 2025-26, the Company maintained its sustained growth trajectory, supported by increased capacities and primarily driven by buoyant demand in the domestic market in the office supplies segment, higher sales of highlighters and ball pens contributed to the overall growth in sales. 

# **Hobby and Craft** 

# **Scholastic Art Material** 

Scholastic Art Material includes products designed to promote creativity, learning, and artistic skill development among students in educational settings. In financial year 2025-26, the segment recorded stable double digit growth. This performance was driven by rising engagement in creative learning activities and the increased adoption of art-based curricula. Furthermore, new product introductions and enhanced brand visibility contributed to improved traction. This momentum was successfully achieved across both domestic and export markets. 

# **Paper Stationery** 

Paper stationery encompasses paper-based products used for writing, drawing, note-taking, and documentation. These items are utilized in academic, professional, and personal settings. The segment registered strong growth during the financial year 2025-26. This success was supported by increased capacities and sustained demand from educational institutions and offices. Additionally, the segment drove inorganic growth through the acquisition of a 51.0% stake in Super Treads Pvt. Ltd., while continuing to expand its existing manufacturing infrastructure. 

# **Kits and Combos** 

Kits and combos consist of curated product bundles designed to offer convenience and value. They also provide comprehensive solutions for specific use cases. This segment emerged as a key growth driver in financial year 2025-26. This success was supported by strong consumer preference for bundled offerings on the back of attractive offerings. 

Hobby and craft items are developed to foster creativity, do-it-yourself activities., and recreational involvement for all ages. The category achieved significant expansion during financial year 2025-26, propelled by numerous product introductions in both white and vibrant colored glues. These highly favored children’s craft adhesives featured appealing, ergonomic bottles tailored for effortless application. This launch was backed by substantial capital deployment into dedicated adhesive production facilities. 

# **Fine Art Products** 

The Fine Art Products segment comprises specialised materials designed to meet the requirements of professional artists and advanced users. The Company continues to focus on offering products with consistent quality, precision and performance, catering to the evolving needs of the fine art community. The segment provides opportunities for portfolio expansion and strengthening the Company’s presence in the premium art materials market. 

# **Back to School Products** 

The strong performance in this category was driven by new launches of pencil boxes and well-designed school bags. These products were introduced in time for the Back to School season. This successful Back to School season launch involved an expanded DOMS-branded school bags range and pencil boxes. The initiative benefited from product and market learnings. These insights were gained during the pilot year in financial year 2025-26. 

# **Hygiene Products** 

Hygiene products include essential items focused on baby diapers and wet wipes. In the financial year 2025-26, the segment continued to scale up operations, supported by increased brand awareness, network expansion and product acceptability backed by enhanced capacities.

# **Human Resources** 

DOMS maintained a permanent workforce of over 13,000 employees as of March 31, 2026. This team drives the Company's daily operations, product innovation, and market expansion. To support them, the Company fosters an inclusive, performance-driven environment focused on professional growth and well-being. The Company actively builds its internal talent pipeline by identifying emerging leaders. It provides equal advancement opportunities and utilizes a leadership succession planning to secure future growth. Structured engagement initiatives are also used to improve productivity and collaboration. To align employee interests with long-term goals, ESOPs were granted to 162 employees across the organisation, this was in addition to the ESOPs granted in 2024-25. Finally, the company continues its commitment to diversity and inclusion. Its workforce includes over 53% of women alongside the employment of more than 38 differently abled individuals.

# **Risk Management** 

The Company’s risk management framework is designed to strengthen governance practices across the organisation through a structured and proactive approach. The risk management policy of DOMS has been formulated in compliance with Regulation 17(9)(b) and Regulation 21 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (‘SEBI LODR Regulations’), as well as the relevant provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 (‘the Act’). 



## **Risk Impact Mitigation strategiesw** 

Non-availability of key raw materials may impact operations. Fluctuations in prices of raw materials including of key raw materials such as polymers, wood, oils, chemicals and pigments **Raw Material** may increase production costs and **Availability and** affect profit margins. **Price Volatility Risk** 

The Company follows a structured procurement approach comprising long-term supplier relationship, periodic price reviews and strategic inventory buffers to manage cost variability and ensure supply continuity. It has strengthened its sourcing strategy by diversifying procurement across multiple suppliers, maintaining multiple sourcing for critical inputs and building strong supplier relationships through better order visibility and payment efficiencies. Backward integration for selected raw materials, such as wood processing, enhances supply reliability and quality control. By continuously strengthening sourcing capabilities and building redundancy across the supply chain, the Company safeguards production continuity, product quality and margins amid market volatility. 

Variability in product quality, colour consistency or writing performance may affect customer experience, brand reputation and product acceptance. 

The Company maintains stringent quality standards across every stage of the manufacturing cycle, including sourcing, processing, manufacturing, packaging and distribution, supported by standardised testing protocols and continuous monitoring. Dedicated quality assurance and quality check teams, ensures compliance with established quality management systems through defined SOPs for inspection of raw and packing materials. In addition, increased in-house product development and manufacturing capabilities have reduced dependence on thirdparty developers, improving operational efficiency and providing greater control over product quality.

# **Internal Control Systems and Their Adequacy** 

The Company has established adequate internal financial controls commensurate with the size and nature of its operations. These controls ensure adherence to policies and procedures, safeguard assets, support fraud detection, maintain accurate accounting records and enable timely preparation of financial reports. The internal control framework aligns with the Company’s corporate governance principles. The Audit Committee periodically reviews the effectiveness of these controls and recommends improvements where necessary.

# **Corporate Governance Report** 

This Corporate Governance Report (‘this Report’) has been prepared in accordance with Regulation 34(3) read with Schedule V of The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (‘SEBI LODR Regulations’) and this Report contains the details of Corporate Governance framework at DOMS Industries Limited (‘DOMS’ or ‘the Company’ or ‘Your Company’). 

## **COMPANY’S PHILOSOPHY ON CODE OF GOVERNANCE** 

Corporate Governance is an integral part of the Company’s operating philosophy and forms the cornerstone of its management and control framework. The Company believes that robust governance practices are essential for safeguarding stakeholder’s interests, ensuring sustainable growth and delivering long-term value. The governance framework adopted by the Company fosters responsible leadership, ethical conduct and effective oversight across all levels of the organization. 

The Company’s Corporate Governance framework ensures transparency in disclosures, accountability in decision making and compliance with applicable laws, regulations and statutory requirements. It reinforces fiduciary responsibility, independent 

judgement and balanced decision making by the Board and the Senior Management of the Company. Through this framework, the Company aims to sustain investor confidence, protect stakeholder interest and uphold the highest standards of corporate integrity. 

Corporate Governance at the Company is viewed not merely as a regulatory requirement, but as a continuous and dynamic process embedded in its business strategy and operations. The Company integrates governance principles into its risk management systems, internal control systems and performance evaluation processes, enabling efficient resource utilization and proactive risk identification and mitigation. 

Consistent adherence to robust governance standards enables the Company to foster trust, accountability and credibility, while reinforcing its commitment to sustainable value creation, regulatory compliance and responsible corporate citizenship. 

Innovation, Commitment and Integrity constitute the core pillars of the Company’s Corporate Governance Philosophy. 

Below are the core values that define how the Company operates and engages with its stakeholders: 

A culture of openness where information is shared transparently and accurately, ensuring that decisions and **Clarity of Conscience:** disclosures are guided by integrity. 

Leadership that views authority as a responsibility, focused on protecting long-term shareholder value, stakeholder **Stewardship Mindset:** trust and the organization’s sustainability. 

A commitment to fairness through continuous oversight of governance practices, safeguarding minority shareholder **Equitable Vigilance:** interests and preventing any form of preferential treatment. 

A framework of responsibility in which leaders are accountable for outcomes and continuously learn, reassess **Adaptive Accountability:** strategies, and evolve in response to changing circumstances or performance gaps.

# **GOVERNANCE STRUCTURE, POLICIES AND PRACTICES** 

The Company operates under a structured 03 (three) level governance framework that delineates authority, responsibility and accountability across all levels of governance. This framework enables effective oversight, informed strategic decision making and disciplined execution of operations, in alignment with established Corporate Governance standards. 

**Board of Directors (‘Board’):** The Board comprises a balanced mix of Executive, Non-Executive and Independent Directors with expertise across diverse domains relevant to our business operations. It provides strategic oversight of the Company’s operations, ensures compliance with applicable legal and regulatory requirements and monitors the execution of business plans. Through their leadership, the Company promotes a culture of integrity, accountability and ethical conduct across the organization, thereby supporting sustainable growth and long-term corporate success. 

strategies and operations are aligned with sustainable growth and long-term value creation. 

# **Board Composition** 

The Board comprises Directors with diverse backgrounds, expertise and experience, enabling balanced perspectives and informed decision-making. This diversity fosters constructive deliberations and strengthens the Board’s ability to provide effective leadership while upholding high standards of corporate governance. The composition of the Board is in compliance with the applicable provisions of The Companies Act, 2013 (‘the Act’) and SEBI LODR Regulations. 

As on March 31, 2026, your Company had 16 (sixteen) Directors, comprising of 01 (one) Managing Director, 04 (four) Whole-time Directors, 03 (three) Non-Executive Directors and 08 (eight) NonExecutive Independent Directors. The Board includes 03 (three) Women Directors, including 01 (one) Woman Independent Director. 

**Committees of Board:** The Board has constituted various committees to oversee specific areas of responsibility, either as required under applicable laws and regulations or as delegated by the Board for focused review and detailed oversight. These Committees facilitate efficient governance and strengthen the Board’s supervisory framework. 

**Executive Management:** The Executive Directors, Senior Management, and Chief Financial Officer (collectively, the ‘Executive Management’) are responsible for the execution of business strategies, operational decision making and providing strategic direction. The Company’s operations and supporting functions, are managed at appropriate levels to ensure effective coordination and control. The Executive Management focuses on achieving the Company’s objectives while driving sustainable growth and long-term value creation. 

# **BOARD OF DIRECTORS** 

At DOMS, the Board is the apex decision making body responsible for providing strategic direction and overseeing the long-term growth and development of the business as well as defining its strategic priorities. 

The Board is committed to the principles of sound corporate governance and plays a crucial role in overseeing management action in alignment with the interests of all the stakeholders, both short term and long term. Its primary objective is to ensure that the Company’s 

**Role of Board of Directors:** 

# **Board Meetings** 

The Board meets at regular intervals to deliberate on the Company’s business policies, strategies, and other matters requiring its consideration. 

The agenda, notes to agenda and requisite supporting documents are generally circulated at least seven days prior to the meeting. All relevant documents/information, including the minimum information required under Regulation 17(7) read with Part A of Schedule II of the SEBI LODR Regulations is made available to the Board. The Agenda for the Board and Committee Meetings is prepared in compliance with the applicable provisions of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations, to the extent applicable. In case of business exigencies or urgency, meetings are convened at a shorter notice with the requisite approvals. Further, in case of a special and urgent business needs, the Board’s approval is taken by passing resolutions through circulation, as permitted by law, which are noted and confirmed in the subsequent Board Meeting. 

The Company complies with the provisions of the Act and the Rules made thereunder, Secretarial Standards 1 issued by The Institute of Company Secretaries of India and SEBI LODR Regulations in relation to the convening and holding the meetings of the Board and its Committees of the Company. The Board being represented by Directors from across the world, it may not be possible for all of them to be present in person at all meetings, therefore the Company provides video conferencing or other audio-visual means facility to enable their seamless participation.

# **Separate Meeting of Independent Directors** 

In order to enable independent, objective, focused and unbiased decision making by the Board in matters relating to the functioning of the Company as well as the Board, Independent Directors are required to meet separately, without the presence of the Non-Independent Directors and the Executive Management of the Company. 

In accordance with the provisions of Schedule IV of the Act and Regulation 25(3) of the SEBI LODR Regulations, the Independent Directors of the Company convened one meeting during the financial year. During this meeting, the Independent Directors, inter alia _,_ reviewed the performance of the Non-Independent Directors and the Board as a whole, evaluated the effectiveness of the Chairperson and examined the adequacy, relevance and timeliness of information provided by the Company’s management to the Board. This process is essential for the Board to perform its duties effectively and responsibly.

# **Familiarization Programme** 

In Compliance with the provisions of SEBI LODR Regulations, the Company has established a structured familiarization program for all the Independent Directors, which includes comprehensive briefings on the following aspects: 

- Profile of the Company 

- Company’s Codes and Key Policies 

- Overview of Company’s Affairs 

- Historical Performance of Company 

- Growth Strategy of the Company 

- Governance and Legal Framework of Company 

The Company proactively apprised the Board on significant business developments and material events through regular electronic communications and other efficient channels. In addition, Directors are provided with quarterly performance summaries, strategic updates and relevant disclosures, including press releases submitted to stock exchanges, ensuring they remain fully informed of all critical developments impacting the Company. 

The details pertaining to Familiarization Programme undertaken by the Company during the financial year 2025-26 is available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia. com/policies/.</u> 

Management Personnel have affirmed adherence to the Code of Conduct, and the Managing Director has provided a declaration confirming such compliance. 

# **BOARD COMMITTEES** 

The Board constituted various committees to ensure focused attention on specific areas and to facilitate informed decision making in the best interests of the Company. These Committees play a pivotal role in strengthening the corporate governance framework and are entrusted with responsibilities as mandated under applicable laws, along with such additional functions as may be delegated by the Board from time to time. As on March 31, 2026, your Company has 05 (five) Committees to the Board. 

All the Committees are appropriately constituted in compliance with the provisions of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations. The composition of these Committees predominantly comprises of Non-Executive Independent Directors, ensuring independence, transparency and objective judgment in their functioning. 

Details of Committees to the Board are as follows: 

1. Audit Committee 

2. Nomination and Remuneration Committee 

3. Stakeholders Relationship Committee 

4. Corporate Social Responsibility Committee and 

# **Code of Conduct** 

The Code of Conduct for Directors and Senior Management is designed to guide the conduct of the Company’s business with honesty, transparency and accountability, while ensuring strict compliance with all applicable laws, rules and regulations. The Code of Conduct for Board of Directors and Senior Management Personnel is available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/policies/. # **Audit Committee** 

The Audit Committee is duly constituted as well as its charter and terms of reference are aligned with the requirements specified under Section 177 of the Act and Regulation 18 read with Part C of Schedule II of the SEBI LODR Regulations. 

The Audit Committee comprises of 06 (six) members. The majority of members of Audit Committee are Non-Executive Independent Directors, who are financially literate and have necessary accounting or financial management expertise. 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Audit Committee held 05 (five) meetings on May 19, 2025, August 08, 2025, November 10, 2025, January 30, 2026 and March 26, 2026. The Composition of Audit Committee along with details of the meeting attended by members, are stated as follows: 

|**Name of Director**|**Category**|**Nature of Membership**|**No. of Meetings**<br>**Entitled to attend**|**No. of Meetings**<br>**Attended**|
|---|---|---|---|---|
|Darshika Thacker|Non-Executive Independent Director|Chairperson|5|5|
|Gianmatteo Terruzzi|Non-Executive Independent Director|Member|5|5|
|Mehul Shah|Non-Executive Independent Director|Member|5|4|
|Rajiv Mistry|Non-Executive Independent Director|Member|5|4|

**90** 

**Financial Statements** 

**Corporate Overview** 

**Statutory Reports** 

The brief terms of reference of Audit Committee, inter alia, includes the following: 

- (a) Oversight of the Company’s financial reporting process and financial statements along with Limited Review/Auditors Report thereon of the Company and its material subsidiary before submission to the Board for approval; 

- (b) Recommendation for appointment, re-appointment and replacement, remuneration and terms of appointment of auditors of the Company and the fixation of audit fee; 

   - (l) Discussion with statutory auditors before the audit commences, about the nature and scope of audit as well as post-audit discussion to ascertain any area of concern; 

   - (m) Reviewing the functioning of the whistle blower and vigil mechanism; 

   - (n) Reviewing the utilization of loans and/or advances from/ investment by the Company in the subsidiary exceeding ₹ 100 crore or 10% of the asset size of the subsidiary, whichever is lower including existing loans/ advances/ investments; 

- (c) Approval of payments to statutory auditors for any other services rendered by the statutory auditors of the Company; 

- (d) Reviewing, with the management, the quarterly financial statements before submission to the board for approval; 

- (e) Monitoring the end use of funds raised through public offers and reviewing, with the management, the statement of uses / application of funds raised through an issue, the statement of funds utilized for purposes other than those stated in the offer document / prospectus / notice and the report submitted by the monitoring agency monitoring the utilization of proceeds of an issue, and making appropriate recommendations to the Board to take up steps in this matter; 

- (o) Considering and commenting on rationale, cost-benefits and impact of schemes involving merger, demerger, amalgamation etc., on the Company and its shareholders; ‘ 

- (p) Carrying out any other functions and roles as provided under the Act, the SEBI LODR Regulations, each as amended and other applicable laws or by any regulatory authority and performing such other functions as may be necessary or appropriate for the performance of its duties. 

The meetings of the Audit Committee are also attended by the Statutory Auditors, Internal Auditor, Chief Financial Officer, Company Secretary & Compliance Officer and other relevant members of the Finance team of the Company. 

- (f) Reviewing and monitoring the auditor’s independence and performance, and effectiveness of audit process; 

- (g) Reviewing, Modification, Approval and Disclosure of Related Party transactions of the Company with related parties and omnibus approval (in the manner specified under the SEBI LODR Regulations and under the Act) for related party transactions proposed to be entered into by the Company; 

The Audit Committee provides assurance of efficiency and effectiveness of operations, both domestic and overseas, reliability of financial and other management information, adequacy of disclosures and compliance with all relevant statutes and regulations. 

The Board has designated Mitesh Padia, Company Secretary & Compliance Officer of the Company, to act as Secretary to the Audit Committee. 

- (h) Scrutiny of inter-corporate loans and investments; 

# **Nomination and Remuneration Committee** 

- (i) Valuation of undertakings or assets of the Company, wherever it is necessary; 

- (j) Evaluation of internal financial controls and risk management systems; 

- (k) Reviewing the adequacy of internal audit function and discussion with internal auditors of any significant findings and follow up there on; 

The Nomination and Remuneration Committee is duly constituted as well as its charter and terms of references are compliant with the requirements specified under Section 178 of the Act and Regulation 19 read with Part D of Schedule II of the SEBI LODR Regulations. 

The Nomination and Remuneration Committee comprises of 03 (three) Members and is responsible to oversee the appointment and remuneration of the Board and Senior Management Personnel and to evaluate their skills, experience, independence and diversity. 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Nomination and Remuneration Committee held 01 (one) meeting on May 19, 2025. The Composition of Nomination and Remuneration Committee along with details of the meeting attended by members, are stated as follows: 

|**Name of Director**|**Category**|**Nature of Membership**|**No. of Meetings**<br>**Entitled to attend**|**No. of Meetings**<br>**attended**|
|---|---|---|---|---|
|Rajiv Mistry|Non-Executive Independent Director|Chairperson|1|1|
|Mehul Shah|Non-Executive Independent Director|Member|1|-|
|Luca Pelosin|Non-Executive Non-Independent Director|Member|1|1|



Annual Report **2025-26 91** 

The brief terms of reference of Nomination and Remuneration Committee, inter alia, includes the following: 

- (a) Formulation of the criteria for determining qualifications, positive attributes and independence of a director and recommend to the Board a policy, relating to the remuneration of the directors, key managerial personnel and other employees; 

- (b) Evaluate the balance of skills, knowledge and experience on the Board and on the basis of such evaluation, prepare a description of the role and capabilities required of an independent director. 

inter alia, included the composition, structured and effectiveness of the Board and its Committees, their role in driving business growth and strategic direction and the proactive initiatives undertaken in the discharge of their responsibilities. The evaluation also considered the collective skill set, professional expertise, leadership qualities and the ability to maintain constructive relationships with stakeholders. 

Further, the evaluation of the Committees covered the timely circulation of agenda materials to facilitate informed deliberations, the quality and impact of key recommendations and action plans, and the extent to which sufficient time and attention were devoted to critical matters prior to approving significant transactions and decisions. 

- (c) Devising a policy on Board diversity; 

- (d) Identifying persons who are qualified to become directors of the Company and who may be appointed in senior management in accordance with the criteria laid down and recommend to the Board their appointment and removal; 

Following key parameters were considered for evaluating the performance of Directors: 

   1. Participation in Board and Committee Meetings 

   2. Managing Relationship 

- (e) Determining the Company’s policy on specific remuneration packages for executive directors including pension rights and any compensation payment, and determining remuneration packages of such directors; 

- (f) Recommending the remuneration, in whatever form, payable to the senior management personnel and other staff (as deemed necessary); 

   3. Knowledge, expertise and Industry Understanding 

   4. Personal Attributes, including leadership 

   5. Effective contribution and application of skills 

   6. Integrity and maintaining of confidentiality 

   7. Independence of judgement and conduct 

   8. Effective management of relationship with stakeholders 

- (g) Perform such functions as are required to be performed by the compensation committee under the Securities and Exchange Board of India (Share Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) Regulations, 2021 and administer, monitor and formulate the Employee Stock Option Plan of the Company; 

- (h) Such terms of reference as may be prescribed under the Act, SEBI LODR Regulations and other applicable laws or by any regulatory authority and performing such other functions as may be necessary or appropriate for the performance of its duties. 

The Nomination and Remuneration Policy of the Company is available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https:// domsindia.com/policies/.</u> 

The Board has designated Mitesh Padia, Company Secretary & Compliance Officer of the Company, to act as Secretary to the Nomination and Remuneration Committee. 

# **Performance Evaluation** 

In compliance with the requirements of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations, the Company has carried out a performance evaluation of the Board, its Committees, the Chairperson and Individual Directors including Independent Directors, during the financial year 2025-26, with an aim to enhance the overall effectiveness of the Board and its Committees. 

The evaluation process was conducted through structured questionnaires covering various aspects of performance. The criteria, 

The Independent Directors, at their separate meeting evaluated the performance of Non-Independent Directors of the Company, the Board as a whole and assessed the timeliness and adequacy of information flow between the Company management and the Board, which is necessary for the Board to effectively and reasonably perform their duties. 

During the financial year 2025-26, a detailed questionnaires were circulated to the members of the Board and Committees to the Board for providing their feedback on the performance of the Board, its Committees and Directors which includes Independent Directors. 

The overall performance evaluation of the Chairperson, the Board, Committees to the Board and Individual Directors was found satisfactory. Suggestions/ feedback concerning strategic, governance and operational matters were duly addressed and actioned by the team.

The Stakeholders Relationship Committee comprises of 03 (three) Members and oversees the complaints received from the shareholders, resolve grievances, performance evaluation of Registrar and Share Transfer Agent (‘RTA’) and various other aspects of interests of stakeholders of the Company.

# **POSTAL BALLOT** 

The Board of Directors at their meeting held on February 03, 2025, approved the Postal Ballot Notice for seeking approval of the Shareholders of the Company by way of Special Resolutions. 

The Postal Ballot Notice along with the Explanatory Statement, was dispatched to the Shareholders of the Company on March 25, 2025, for the purpose of obtaining their approval, inter alia, for the following matters: 

1. Special Resolutions: 

   - a. Approval of Special Rights Granted to Promoter and Promoter Group of the Company under the Shareholders’ Agreement 

- b. Approval for increasing the maximum number of Directors of the Company from the existing limit of 15 (fifteen) Directors to 20 (twenty) Directors 

- c. Approval for altering and adapting the revised set of Articles of Association of the Company 

In compliance with Regulation 44 of SEBI LODR Regulations, Sections 108, 110 and other applicable provisions of the Act read with Rules 20 and 22 of the Companies (Management and Administration) Rules, 2014 and the General Circulars issued in this regard by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (‘MCA’), the Company provided a remote e-voting facility to all its Shareholders to vote on the aforementioned resolutions. 

The Company engaged the services of National Securities Depository Limited (‘NSDL’) to provide the remote e-voting facility to all its Shareholders.

# **MEANS OF COMMUNICATION** 

The Company believes that clear, accurate and timely dissemination of information to its Shareholders is fundamental to ensuring transparency and strengthening corporate governance. In view of this, the Company disseminates information through various communication channels, including filings made with the Stock Exchanges, press releases, Annual Reports and updates published on its official website. The Company ensures timely disclosure of 

The quarterly, half-yearly and annual financial results of the Company are duly filed with the Stock Exchanges, in accordance with the SEBI LODR Regulations. The said financial results are also published in the ‘Financial Express’ (English) newspaper which has nationwide circulation and in ‘Damanganga Times’ or ‘Gujarat Guardian’ (Gujarati) newspaper where the registered office of the Company is situated. The financial results are also available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/financial-statements/.</u> 

## **Website** 

All the requisite information and disclosures as required under Regulation 46 of SEBI LODR Regulations are available under the Investor Relations tab as ‘Disclosure under Regulation 46 of SEBI LODR Regulations’ that provides information about various announcements and disclosures made by the Company and can be accessed at <u>https:// domsindia.com/disclosure-under-regulation-46-of-sebi-lodr/.</u> 

## **News Releases** 

All official news/ press releases issued by the Company are duly filed with the Stock Exchanges in accordance with the SEBI LODR Regulations. The same are also available on the website of the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/ press-release-2/.</u> 

## **Presentations to institutional investors/ analysts** 

The Company conducts conference calls with Institutional Investors/ Analysts after declaration of financial results to apprise them on the Company’s business performance and key developments. In compliance with Regulation 46 of SEBI LODR Regulations the 

Annual Report **2025-26 97** 

presentations, video/ audio recordings and transcript of the meetings are filed with stock exchanges and are also available on the website of - the Company and can be accessed at <u>https://domsindia.com/investor presentation-and-transcripts/.</u> 

No Unpublished Price Sensitive Information is shared/ discussed during the interactions with institutional investors and financial analysts. 

### **SEBI Complaints Redress System (SCORES)** 

The SCORES Platform of SEBI is a centralised web-based grievance redressal system that facilitates investors to lodge complaints online and track the status update of their grievances in a transparent and time bound manner. The Company is committed to addressing investor grievances diligently and undertakes to resolve all complaints received through SCORES promptly, in coordination with the concerned regulatory authorities. 

### **Online Dispute Resolution Portal (ODR Portal)** 

Pursuant to the SEBI Circular dated July 31, 2023, shareholders may resolve their grievances through the Online Dispute Resolution mechanism introduced by SEBI through a common ODR Portal. Shareholders are advised to first lodge their grievances, if any, with MUFG Intime India Private Limited (Formerly known as Link Intime India Private Limited), Registrar and Share Transfer Agent of the Company, by sending an email to investor.helpdesk@in.mpms.mufg. <u>com. In case the grievance remains unresolved or the shareholder</u> is not satisfied with the resolution provided, the shareholder may escalate the matter through the SEBI Complaints Redress System portal in accordance with the applicable SCORES guidelines. If the shareholder is still not satisfied with the outcome, online dispute resolution may be initiated through the ODR Portal at <u>https:// smartodr.in/login.</u> 

## **Designated exclusive email-Id** 

To better serve investors and in accordance with the provisions of SEBI LODR Regulations, the designated email address for investor grievances is <u>ir@domsindia.com. The same is available on the website</u> of the Company and can be accessed at <u>www.domsindia.com.</u> 

## **NSE Electronic Application Processing System (NEAPS) and BSE Corporate Compliance & the Listing Centre** 

NEAPS and BSE Listing Centre are web-based applications developed by NSE and BSE, respectively, for corporates to make electronic submissions. All periodical compliance filings and other material information as specified under SEBI LODR Regulations are submitted electronically through these platforms. Further, in compliance with the provisions of the SEBI LODR Regulations, all the disclosures made to the Stock Exchanges are in a format that enables users to easily locate relevant information through search tool. 

# **OTHER DISCLOSURES** 

## **1. Disclosure on materially significant Related Party Transactions** 

The Company has formulated and adopted a Policy on Related Party Transactions in compliance with the requirements of the Act and SEBI LODR Regulations and is available on the website of the Company https://domsindia.com/policies/. 

All Related Party Transactions entered by the Company were placed before the Audit Committee for their review and approval. Prior omnibus approval was obtained from the Audit Committee for all the related party transactions which are repetitive in nature and/or entered in the ordinary course of business and are at arm’s length. 

All Related Party Transactions entered during the year were in ordinary course of business and on arm’s length basis and were in compliance with the provisions as set out in the Act read with the Rules issued thereunder and relevant provisions of the SEBI LODR Regulations. 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company did not enter into any material Related Party Transactions that may have potential conflict with interest of the Company at large. 

The disclosure as required under Indian Accounting Standard (Ind AS) on ‘Related Party Disclosures’ is disclosed in Note No. 43 of the Standalone Financial Statements of the Company. 

## **2. Disclosure of Non-compliances** 

During the financial year 2025-26, there were no instances of non-compliance by the Company. Further, no penalties or strictures were imposed on the Company by SEBI, Stock Exchanges where the Equity Shares of the Company are listed or any other statutory authority, on matters relating to capital markets during the last three financial years. 

## **3. Vigil Mechanism and Whistle Blower Policy** 

The Company is committed to fostering a culture of integrity, transparency and ethical conduct in all its business dealings. In furtherance of this commitment, it has established a Whistle Blower and Vigil Mechanism Policy, which provides employees with a formal and confidential channel to report concerns in a transparent and accountable manner. This framework enables employees to highlight instances of suspected misconduct, unlawful acts, fraudulent practices, or any other breach of applicable policies or regulations. 

The Policy ensures adequate safeguards against any form of retaliation for employees who use this mechanism. It also provides direct access to the Chairperson of the Audit Committee for submitting complaints in suitable and exceptional circumstances. 

During the financial year 2025-26, no such instances were reported and no person was denied access to the Chairperson of Audit Committee of the Company with regards to above. 

The Whistle Blower and Vigil Mechanism Policy of the Company is available on the Website of the Company and same can be accessed at https://domsindia.com/policies/. 

## **4. Policy Determining Material Subsidiaries** 

The Company has adopted a Policy for Determination of Material Subsidiary in terms of the requirements of the SEBI LODR Regulations. Pioneer Stationery Private Limited is a material subsidiary of the Company and has adopted the requirements as specified in the Policy of the Company. The Audit Committee regularly reviews the financial statements and business performance of the subsidiary companies.

# **SHARE TRANSFER SYSTEM** 

In accordance with the provisions of the Regulation 40(1) of SEBI LODR Regulations and directions issued by the Securities Exchange Board of India (‘SEBI’) with effect from April 01, 2019, requests for transfer of securities are not processed unless the securities are held in dematerialised form. 

All the matters relating to the Stakeholders of the Company, including transfer and transmission of securities are reviewed and approved by the Stakeholders Relationship Committee of the Company. 

As mandated by the SEBI, securities of the Company can be transferred/ traded only in dematerialised form. As on March 31, 2026, entire shareholding of the Company is in Dematerialised form with National Securities Depository Limited (‘NSDL’) and Central Depository Services (India) Limited (‘CDSL’).

# **OUTSTANDING INSTRUMENTS AND THEIR IMPACT ON EQUITY** 

During the financial year 2025-26, the Company has not issued any Global Depository Receipts (GDR) or American Depository Receipts (ADR) or warrants or any other convertible instruments, which are convertible into Equity Shares of the Company. 

# **COMMODITY PRICE RISKS OR FOREIGN EXCHANGE RISK AND COMMODITY HEDGING ACTIVITIES** 

The Company uses plantation wood to make its wooden pencils. It also imports some other raw materials from other countries. Changes in weather conditions and issues in international trade may affect the supply and prices of these materials. However, the Company has long-standing relationships with its suppliers and keeps enough stock of these materials to handle any short-term supply problems.

# **E-VOTING** 

E-voting is a digital platform that enables shareholders to participate in the decision-making process of a company by casting their votes 

electronically on proposed resolutions. In compliance with the applicable statutory provisions, shareholders are provided with the facility to exercise their voting rights on matters that require approval through Postal Ballot by electronic means, thereby eliminating the need for physical dispatch of ballot papers. 

The Company has also provided the facility of e-voting for the resolutions proposed to be considered at the forthcoming Annual General Meeting. The Ministry of Corporate Affairs has designated National Securities Depository Limited (NSDL) and Central Depository Services (India) Limited (CDSL) as authorised agencies for providing electronic voting infrastructure. The Company has accordingly entered into an arrangement with NSDL to facilitate e-voting for its shareholders. 

# **CREDIT RATINGS** 

The Company has engaged CRISIL Ratings Limited as its Credit Rating Agency. During the financial year 2025-26, CRISIL upgraded the Company’s credit rating for its bank loan facilities from ‘CRISIL A+/ Stable’ to ‘CRISIL AA-/Stable’.

# **Dear Stakeholders,** 

Sustainable growth has been one of the core focus areas of our vision. This year too, I am happy to report that our Company has made good progress across key sustainability parameters. 

equal opportunity for all. Our workplaces are accessible to differently abled employees and workers, and we remain focused on creating an environment where everyone is treated with fairness and respect. 

# **Environmental Stewardship** 

The Company continued its efforts to improve environmental performance across its operations. We continued the use of solar power at our Umbergaon facilities and strengthened our focus on responsible resource management. We continued to reuse production residues, recycle plastic waste through authorised partners and improve wastewater treatment and recycling infrastructure. We also progressed towards the use of more sustainable packaging solutions while ensuring compliance with applicable environmental regulations. 

# **Our People, our Strength** 

As at the end of this financial year, our Company employed over 12,000 employees and workers, including contract workers. We continue to provide a safe and inclusive workplace supported by regular health and safety training, employee welfare measures and 

# **Governance at the Center of Operations** 

We have always considered governance at the forefront of all our activities. We continue to make incremental improvements in our processes, controls and systems to build resilience and protect against risks. Various members of the Board, key managerial personnel and other senior managerial personnel have been tasked with leading matters on regulatory compliance, occupational health and safety, human resource management and development, risk management, IT security and controls, among other sustainability-linked matters. The Company’s Stakeholder Engagement Policy also helps to protect stakeholder interests and instate formal grievance redressal mechanisms. 

Warm Regards, **Santosh Raveshia** Managing Director

## **b. What is the contribution of exports as a percentage of the total turnover of the entity?** 

The contribution of exports as a percentage of total turnover of the Company is 12%. The Company’s major export markets include the United States, Italy, Nepal, and Canada. 

## **c. A brief on types of customers** 

DOMS is engaged in the business of manufacturing and marketing scholastic stationery, scholastic art materials, paper stationery, office supplies, hobby and craft products, fine arts products, back to school products as well as kits and combo packs. Our products cater to consumers across all ages and segments - kids, students, young adults, office professionals, artists and creative enthusiasts. 

Our products are primarily sold under the flagship brand ‘DOMS’, as well as through other brands/ sub-brands like C3, Amariz, and FixyFix. 

We primarily conduct our business through the following channels: 

## **i. Domestic Network:** 

In India, our products are distributed through a robust and well-established network of super stockists, distributors, and retailers. Additionally, we reach consumers via modern trade outlets, retail chains, e-commerce platforms, and quickcommerce channels. 

## **ii. Export Network:** 

In global markets, we operate through merchants and trading houses to ensure wide accessibility of our products. Our international sales are also facilitated through FILA and FILA Group companies network in regions where they have a presence.

**4. Does the entity have an anti-corruption or anti-bribery policy? If yes, provide details in brief and if available, provide a web-link to the policy.** 

   - The Company has established frameworks such as the Code of Conduct and Ethics, Code of Conduct for Directors and Senior Management, and the Responsible Supply Chain Policy, which incorporate guidelines on anti-corruption and anti-bribery. These provisions are applicable to all Directors, employees, and value chain partners in relation to their association with the Company. The policies are available at <u>https:// domsindia.com/policies/.</u> 

We are committed to integrity and transparency, and do not allow any corrupt practices. The Company’s policy strictly prohibits offering, accepting, or soliciting bribes, kickbacks, or facilitation payments from or to competitors, vendors, suppliers, customers, or any other parties engaged or intending to engage in business with the Company. 

The policy framework reasonably regulates exchange of gifts, contributions, and hospitality in the course of business interactions. The Company endeavours to ensure that all dealings are conducted on an arm’s length basis and remain free from any influence that could compromise objectivity or decision-making. 

The Company complies with the provisions of the Prevention of Corruption Act, 1988 and adheres to equivalent legal requirements in jurisdictions where it conducts international operations.

# **Principle 2** 

**Businesses should provide goods and services in a manner that is sustainable and safe.** 

## **Essential Indicators** 

**1. Percentage of R&D and capital expenditure (capex) investments in specific technologies to improve the environmental and social impacts of product and processes to total R&D and capex investments made by the entity, respectively.** 

||||(Hin lakhs)|
|---|---|---|---|
|**Particulars**|**FY 2025-26**|**FY 2024-25**|**Details of improvements in**<br>**environmental and social impacts**|
|R&D|-|-|Refer note below|
|Capex|216.70|558.68|Refer note below|
||1.35%|4.22%||



### **Note:** 

### **Research and Development Process:** 

The Company’s research and development efforts are focused on designing products that are safe, non-toxic, user-friendly, economical, and capable of minimizing wastage. Over the years, the Company has continuously enhanced its practices and introduced several initiatives aimed at generating positive environmental and social outcomes. These initiatives include the adoption of recycled materials, in-house recycling practices, adherence to product safety standards, and periodic reassessment of products. 

The Company also undertakes regular Life Cycle Assessments (LCA) of its products to improve product safety and reduce environmental impact. In addition, initiatives have been undertaken to gradually replace plastic/PVC-based packaging with eco-friendly paper-based packaging alternatives, wherever feasible. 

As these initiatives have evolved as part of the Company’s continuous operational and product improvement processes, no separate research and development expenditure is specifically attributable to such activities. 

### **Capital Expenditure:** 

The Company has incurred capital expenditure towards fire safety systems, sewage treatment plants, water sanitation facilities, and electrical infrastructure across its facilities. These investments enhance workplace safety, improve operational efficiency, and strengthen emergency preparedness. Further, the wastewater treatment and water management systems support environmental compliance and resource conservation. Overall, these initiatives contribute towards improving the environmental and social impact of the Company’s operations and processes. 

**2. a. Does the entity have procedures in place for sustainable sourcing? (Yes/No)** 

   - Yes, the Company has adopted a Responsible Supply Chain Policy (https://domsindia.com/policies/), which is applicable to all regular suppliers and vendors. To facilitate effective implementation, relevant clauses have been incorporated into purchase orders and new contractual agreements. 

The Company also undertakes appropriate due diligence while onboarding new partners and continues to monitor existing partners to evaluate any instances of non-compliance with the policy. 

- **b. If yes, what percentage of inputs were sourced sustainably?** 

100%. All our recurring vendors and suppliers are covered under the Responsible Supply Chain Policy.
